# OT1997 chunk 4 — Compact entering-law selections

Exact selected complete case blocks copied from current canonical Holdings volumes. Full selected-volume tool copy: [OT_1997CHUNK4.md](OT_1997CHUNK4.md). This reading aid is not authority. Effective current-term law follows below; uncoordinated same-day peers share one pre-group baseline.

## Source: [state/holdings/constitutional-structure.md](../../../state/holdings/constitutional-structure.md)

#### United States v. Lopez

**Docket or dockets:** No. 93-1260.
**Decided:** April 26, 1995.

##### The Commerce Clause requires a constitutionally identifiable connection before aggregate magnitude can establish federal authority

**Holding:** When Congress regulates conduct that is neither interstate commerce itself nor a channel, instrumentality, person, or thing in interstate commerce, the Court must identify the regulated statutory class and the interstate mechanism connecting that class to commerce among the several States before aggregation and substantiality may perform constitutional work. Connection before magnitude: aggregation may measure the effects of a class already connected to interstate commerce; it may not manufacture the missing constitutional connection. Congress may regulate a properly defined class of economic conduct materially participating in or affecting an actual interstate market, but generalized downstream consequences through crime, education, health, insurance, consumption, productivity, or public expenditure cannot themselves supply the missing connection. The original §922(q)(1)(A) school-zone possession prohibition fails at the connection stage: it regulates no interstate transaction, channel, instrumentality, economic class, or identified anti-evasion component of a valid interstate regulatory regime, and the chain from local firearm possession through crime costs, impaired education and reduced productivity to the national economy cannot supply that connection.

**Proposition-level authority:** Chief Justice Stone-Zsela's Opinion of the Court, joined by Justices O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, and Thomas: five Justices.

**Controlling explanation:** The Court's sequence is: (1) identify the regulated statutory class; (2) identify the asserted Commerce connection or anchor; (3) identify the interstate mechanism that connects that class to commerce among the several States; (4) only after such a constitutionally sufficient connection exists may aggregation and substantiality determine the magnitude of the class-wide effect; (5) Congress receives substantial latitude over factual prediction and regulatory design once it acts inside the enumerated power; (6) there must be a reasonable, judicially identifiable fit between the class Congress actually regulated and the mechanism invoked; and (7) a proposed theory must leave an enforceable boundary between the Commerce power and a general police power, so generalized downstream consequences cannot themselves supply the missing connection. The Court independently determines whether the asserted mechanism is constitutionally adequate. The fit requirement is not strict scrutiny or a least-restrictive-means test, and the boundary inquiry is a diagnostic of the asserted mechanism, not an independent balancing factor. The rule is not an economic/noneconomic categorical switch, a traditional-governmental-function immunity, a mandatory-legislative-findings rule, a closed list of metaphysical Commerce categories, or a rule that every individual instance must substantially affect interstate commerce.

**Material application:** The regulated class is knowing firearm possession in a defined school zone. It is not paid firearm delivery, interstate trafficking, interstate shipment or participation in the commercial firearms market merely because the record contains evidence that Lopez expected $40 for delivery or that the handgun had previously been manufactured outside Texas. The Court accepts that violent crime carries economic costs and need not dispute every empirical step of the education-and-productivity chain; neither theory explains why this statutory class constitutes commerce among the States, and neither leaves an enforceable stopping point.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Wickard v. Filburn, 317 U.S. 111: preserves aggregation at its economic-class scope; the school-possession/productivity chain does not extend that holding to this stand-alone offense.
- Perez v. United States, 402 U.S. 146: preserves Congress’s regulation of an economic class without requiring each instance to have a substantial effect; its regulated commercial setting remains material.
- Allied-Bruce Terminix Cos. v. Dobson, January 18, 1995: distinguishes actual interstate commercial operation from remote-object history; its statutory breadth and reserved constitutional ceiling remain intact.
- Hodel v. Virginia Surface Mining & Reclamation Assn., 452 U.S. 264: preserves broad review of congressional commercial regulation without treating deference as a grant of general police power or imposing a universal formal-findings requirement.
- New York v. United States, June 19, 1992: distinguishes compelled state administration from direct regulation of private persons; neither its anti-compulsion rule nor the traditional local subject alone decides this case.
- Garcia v. San Antonio Metropolitan Transit Authority, 469 U.S. 528: remains undisturbed in its generally applicable regulation of state activity; traditional-governmental-function immunity is not revived.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No categorical exclusion of noneconomic intrastate conduct, reserved State subject area, universal formal-findings requirement or rule that only conduct physically crossing State lines is regulable is adopted. Findings may support legislative facts concerning an identified interstate mechanism, but they cannot create the constitutional connection. No particular form of jurisdictional element is held automatically sufficient. The holding creates no Second Amendment rule and decides no amended possession statute, separate discharge offense or materially different federal firearms offense containing a commerce element.

##### Intrastate conduct necessary to a valid interstate regulatory regime remains potentially regulable

**Holding:** Congress may sometimes include intrastate conduct where inclusion is genuinely necessary and proper to prevent evasion or material impairment of an otherwise valid interstate regulatory regime. That authority derives from the Commerce Clause together with the Necessary and Proper Clause and requires an identifiable relationship between the local class and the valid interstate regime. It cannot save original §922(q)(1)(A): the Government identifies no national firearms-market prohibition whose operation materially depends on criminalizing all possession within 1,000 feet of schools, and the enacted offense does not define its class around concealment, retention, acquisition, trafficking or another mechanism by which school-zone possession undermines a larger federal market regime.

**Proposition-level authority:** Chief Justice Stone-Zsela's Opinion of the Court, joined by Justices O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, and Thomas: five Justices.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court recognizes this route without fully defining it; fuller definition of its requirements is reserved for a case that actually presents it. Lopez does not formulate a separate interstate-externality doctrine: whether materially different intrastate conduct imposing direct and substantial interstate spillovers that individual States cannot practically confine or internalize may be reached remains reserved for a later case actually presenting it.

**Later-authority backlinks:** United States v. Hale, February 12, 1996, Eighth Circuit No. 91-3830, supplies the first operational rule for the reserved anti-evasion/material-impairment route: Congress may include possession within §922(o)'s direct prohibition of additions to the post-May 19, 1986 civilian machinegun market when excluding it would materially impair enforcement of that valid transfer/stock regime; see Constitutional Structure — Federal regulation of economic activity. The interstate-externality reservation is unchanged.

##### The original possession text cannot be saved by adding a new commerce element

**Holding:** The Court will not insert an unexpressed commerce element into original §922(q)(1)(A) to sustain this charge. Constitutional avoidance permits a fair narrowing construction of enacted language; it does not permit the judiciary to enact a different federal offense. The stipulated facts that the handgun was manufactured outside Texas and that Lopez allegedly expected $40 for delivery do not become statutory elements. Because no commerce element is judicially inserted and the constitutional ground requires affirmance of dismissal under the enacted prohibition, the Court does not decide the separate grand-jury question that would arise if such an element were created and then supported only by trial evidence rather than an indictment allegation.

**Proposition-level authority:** Chief Justice Stone-Zsela's Opinion of the Court, joined by Justices O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, and Thomas: five Justices.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- United States v. Bass, 404 U.S. 336: preserves federalism-sensitive construction of ambiguous firearms language, without authorizing addition of an element absent from this unambiguous provision.
- Gregory v. Ashcroft, 501 U.S. 452: preserves its clarity principle within permissible statutory interpretation; it supplies no power to rewrite this offense.
- United States v. X-Citement Video, Inc., November 29, 1994: distinguishes interpretation of an enacted knowledge term from adding a new jurisdictional condition; its separate rules for defending a favorable judgment remain intact.
- Stirone v. United States, 361 U.S. 212: supplies the grand-jury allegation boundary invoked against substituting trial proof for a necessary indictment allegation; the renewed objection remains conditional and is not decided after affirmance on another sufficient ground.
- Russell v. United States, 369 U.S. 749: supplies the grand-jury allegation boundary invoked against curing omission of a necessary offense allegation through later proof; that conditional indictment objection is left unresolved because the commerce ground is sufficient.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The Fifth Circuit's reversal of the original §922(q)(1)(A) conviction and dismissal mandate are affirmed. No prosecution reinstatement, new commerce allegation, sentencing decision or merits remand is ordered. Supervised-release, special-assessment and conditional indictment objections are unnecessary to decide.

## Source: [state/holdings/constitutional-structure.md](../../../state/holdings/constitutional-structure.md)

#### United States v. Hale, judgment below 978 F.2d 1016

**Docket or dockets:** Eighth Circuit No. 91-3830; no separate Supreme Court docket supplied.

**Decided:** February 12, 1996.

##### Congress may include possession in this regulation of the machinegun market

**Holding:** Congress may include possession within §922(o)'s direct prohibition of additions to the post-May 19, 1986 civilian machinegun market, subject to the subsection's government-related and prior-lawful-possession exceptions, when possession and transfer are sufficiently integrated that excluding possession would permit acquisition, retention, concealment, or continued circulation of the prohibited stock and thereby materially impair enforcement of the valid transfer/stock regime. The relationship is one of anti-evasion and material impairment under the Commerce Clause together with the Necessary and Proper Clause, and it defeats the presented commerce challenge without a transaction-specific interstate element. General gun violence, fear, insurance cost, marketability alone, or remote interstate history does not supply the ground.

**Proposition-level authority:** O'Connor's Part I, unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Section 922(o)(2) excepts transfers to or by, and possession by or under the authority of, the United States or its departments or agencies, a State or its departments, agencies or political subdivisions; it separately excepts lawful transfer or possession of a machinegun lawfully possessed before May 19, 1986. Earlier manufacture alone does not satisfy that second exception. No count is newly found within either exception.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Lopez is clarified and developed: Lopez reserved intrastate inclusion genuinely necessary to prevent evasion or material impairment of a valid interstate regime, and this decision supplies the first operational rule for that route. Wickard and Perez are preserved for economic-class aggregation. Harris, Bishop/Stokes and American Life League rest on distinct mechanisms; none is converted into the source of Hale's anti-evasion rule, and no other statute's interstate element is transferred into §922(o).

## Source: [state/holdings/constitutional-structure.md](../../../state/holdings/constitutional-structure.md)

#### Fulton Corp. v. Faulkner, 516 U.S. 325

**Docket or dockets:** No. 94-1239.

**Decided:** February 21, 1996.

##### The issuer-based deduction discriminates against interstate investment

**Holding:** A State discriminates against interstate commerce when the tax on a resident's otherwise comparable corporate shares increases as the issuer's local income-tax exposure decreases. Neither the shareholder's local residence nor drafting the geographic advantage as a deduction removes that discrimination, and proof that a particular issuer already lost an investment is unnecessary.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Thomas), unanimous.

**Material application:** The tax is twenty-five cents per $100 of share value, reduced according to the issuer's North Carolina-apportioned income. For corporate shareholders the linked dividend-deduction proportion applies without the ordinary $15,000 dividend-deduction cap.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Oregon Waste's express-geographic-discrimination rule and West Lynn's examination of linked operation apply; a deduction label or shareholder residence does not remove discrimination.

##### Corporate income and share ownership are not shown to be equivalent taxable events

**Holding:** A compensatory-tax defense requires the State to identify the actual intrastate burden being offset, show a roughly equivalent and nonexcessive interstate charge, and establish substantially equivalent taxable events reasonably treated as mutually exclusive proxies. North Carolina's corporate-income and shareholder-property taxes do not satisfy those requirements merely because corporate profits may influence share value; the failure to establish equivalent events independently defeats the defense.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Thomas), unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Different nominal taxpayers do not invariably prevent compensation, and income and property taxes may coexist. This State failed to establish equivalent events.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Oregon Waste supplies the cumulative compensatory conditions, Maryland v. Louisiana the actual-local-burden requirement and Armco the independently fatal equivalent-events defect. Henneford preserves genuinely complementary taxation.

**Independent alternative holding:** A State does not establish the compensatory defense's actual-burden and approximate-equivalence requirements merely by invoking typical price/earnings ratios or aggregate corporate tax contributions. North Carolina's comparison fails to demonstrate the required matching burden on this record, an additional ground for rejecting its defense.

**Proposition-level authority for alternative holding:** Opinion of the Court (Thomas), unanimous.

**Limits, remedy, transition, or precedent treatment:** The 1990 claim is not governed by a new universal calculation using the later 7.75% rate; the state discussion also identifies the earlier 7% rate. Its general price/earnings reasoning is neither a statutory command nor an adopted equality finding for every taxpayer. Associated Industries requires the relevant actual matching burden, rather than aggregate or other-transaction offsets.

##### Darnell's contrary Commerce Clause permission is overruled

**Holding:** Darnell v. Indiana is overruled to the extent it permits discriminatory taxation of residents' shares in out-of-state corporations under the Commerce Clause merely by reference to a different corporate-level local tax and an asserted substantial equality. Its distinct equal-protection and standing determinations are not erased.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Thomas), unanimous.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Darnell v. Indiana, 226 U.S. 390, is expressly overruled only on its incompatible Commerce Clause permission. Oregon Waste and Associated Industries require the cumulative inquiry; Darnell's separate standing and equal-protection determinations remain.

##### Invalidity requires lawful remedial proceedings, not a predetermined refund

**Holding:** Reversal of the tax's constitutional justification requires remand for state-law severability and constitutionally adequate relief. When a State compelled payment before a meaningful opportunity to contest the tax, McKesson requires adequate backward-looking correction; independently, Reich forbids withdrawing a clearly held-out postpayment remedy after payment and substituting an unpursued prepayment route.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Thomas), unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** McKesson permits lawful refunds, constitutionally permissible equalizing retrospective treatment or a lawful combination where backward-looking relief is required. Reich's protection applies to a clearly held-out postpayment remedy even after voluntary payment; reasonable previously announced conditions and prospective changes with fair notice remain permissible.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Reverse and remand for severability and constitutionally adequate relief according to actual payments, preservation, procedures and lawful state choices. No fixed refund, fee award, interest, assessment against others, blanket stock exemption, deduction change or automatic reinstatement of the intermediate appellate remedy follows.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** McKesson's conditional backward-looking duty and Reich's independent no-withdrawal protection govern relief without selecting a universal refund formula.

## Source: [state/holdings/constitutional-structure.md](../../../state/holdings/constitutional-structure.md)

#### Walmer v. Department of Defense

**Docket or dockets:** Tenth Circuit No. 93-3377.

**Decided:** January 13, 1997.

For the other questions in Walmer v. Department of Defense, Tenth Circuit No. 93-3377, decided January 13, 1997, see Civil Rights - Military personnel and equal protection (Ordinary rationality sustains this preliminary denial on the stipulated conduct record).

##### The attainder prohibition does not extend to this executive military regulation

**Holding:** The Bill of Attainder Clause restrains legislative punishment, and the challenged executive Army regulation does not satisfy that legislative-action predicate. The attainder ground therefore fails without deciding whether the separation would constitute punishment if the required legislative predicate existed; other constitutional, statutory and administrative restraints on executive action remain distinct.

**Proposition-level authority:** O'Connor's Part II, unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Punishment is not reached; honorable separation is not held conclusively nonpunitive. The abandoned APA theory and unpresented due-process or statutory claims are not revived.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Nixon, Selective Service System and Ambassador Books supply and preserve the legislative-action prerequisite. Their distinct punishment inquiries are neither displaced nor answered for this separation.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Unanimously reject the attainder ground for want of legislative action; no punishment determination.

## Source: [state/holdings/constitutional-structure.md](../../../state/holdings/constitutional-structure.md)

#### Camps Newfound/Owatonna, Inc. v. Town of Harrison

**Docket or dockets:** No. 94-1988.
**Decided:** 1997-05-19.

##### Paid interstate camp services remain protected against discriminatory taxation

**Holding:** A charitable camp that recruits nationally and sells services to visitors crossing state lines participates in interstate commerce, although its property and performance are local and it is nonprofit. Where the same otherwise qualifying private camp offering receives worse tax treatment because its principal beneficiaries are nonresidents, that express geographic condition discriminates; a claimed resident-service distinction must rest on the actual relationship or offering, not beneficiary residence alone.

**Proposition-level authority:** Breyer's Parts I–II, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Souter; six direct joins.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The nonresident subparagraph grants at most $50,000 in exempt current just property value only if the statutory average weekly charge is no more than $30; higher charges receive no exemption under that subparagraph. The average divides tax-year charges by all recipients and by the weeks operated principally for nonresidents; the advertised weekly price is not that calculation. Nonprofit corporations incorporated solely for medical research are excepted from this subparagraph, not from neutral general qualifications. Neither hypothetical exception eligibility, Maine-incorporation validity, all charitable acts, religious liberty, equal protection nor travel rights is decided.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Heart of Atlanta treats interstate travel for local services as commerce; Fulton scrutinizes geographic tax conditions despite local property or an exemption form. Tracy requires actual offering/service obligations; its regulated rate, reliability and service duties are absent here, but no utility-style regulation is made universally indispensable. Carbone preserves comparison of the actual private service and distinct genuine public systems.

##### Resident benefit, subsidy and public-capacity labels do not justify this tax condition

**Holding:** This discriminatory private-clientele tax condition can survive only upon a legitimate local purpose that reasonable nondiscriminatory alternatives cannot adequately serve; support for resident charity does not establish that showing here. Forgone taxes do not make Maine a purchaser of the camp's services or remove the discriminatory tax condition from review, and a compensatory-tax defense requires an identified intrastate burden, a roughly equivalent nonexcessive charge and substantially equivalent taxable events, none established by general resident contributions or asserted charitable savings.

**Proposition-level authority:** Breyer's Parts I–II, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Souter; six direct joins.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The decision leaves direct resident benefits, ordinary general-revenue assistance, neutral charitable criteria and actual purchased services available. It requires no worldwide charitable support, presumes no numerical disproportion or default, and imposes no duty to disprove every imagined alternative.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Oregon Waste supplies demanding local-purpose/nondiscriminatory-alternative scrutiny. Fulton and Oregon Waste require the identified intrastate burden, rough equivalent nonexcessive charge and substantially equivalent taxable events for compensation. SSC protects actual purchased performance; forgone revenue establishes none. West Lynn examines practical fiscal operation without requiring its dedicated-fund facts.

##### Reversal requires lawful state relief, not an automatic exemption or fixed refund

**Holding:** The judgment sustaining this discriminatory condition must be reversed and the case remanded for state-law severability, remaining neutral exemption conditions and constitutionally adequate relief. If payment was compelled before meaningful review, adequate backward-looking correction is required; independently, a clearly held-out postpayment remedy may not be withdrawn after payment and replaced by an unpursued prepayment route, even where payment was voluntary, subject to reasonable preannounced conditions and fair prospective changes.

**Proposition-level authority:** Breyer's Part III, joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy and Souter; five direct votes. Stone-Zsela requires the exemption and does not join this open remedial choice; Scalia, Thomas and Ginsburg would affirm.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Reverse the residence-condition validation, 6–3, and remand under the five-Justice state-law remedy instruction. Required correction may take a lawful refund, constitutionally permissible retrospective equalization or combination; no fixed refund, interest, new assessment, substitute subsidy or unconditional exemption is ordered.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Fulton separates invalidity from lawful state remedial choices. McKesson requires backward correction when payment preceded meaningful review; Reich independently protects a clearly held-out postpayment route even after voluntary payment, subject to reasonable preannounced conditions and fair prospective change.

## Source: [state/holdings/constitutional-structure.md](../../../state/holdings/constitutional-structure.md)

#### Clinton v. Jones, 520 U.S. 681 (1997)

**Docket or dockets:** No. 95-1853.
**Decided:** 1997-05-27.

For connected appellate review and the invalid categorical trial stay, see Federal Courts — Presidential immunity appeals and trial stays, Clinton v. Jones, May 27, 1997, No. 95-1853.

##### No temporary immunity for the presented pre-presidential conduct

**Holding:** A sitting President has no constitutional temporary immunity from a properly brought federal civil action seeking damages for alleged unofficial conduct before taking office. The Presidency alone neither requires dismissal without prejudice nor suspends that action until the term ends; concrete demands upon official duties require their own accommodation analysis.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; eight direct rationale votes. Breyer concurs in the judgment only.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The holding concerns a properly brought federal damages action for alleged unofficial pre-office conduct. It decides no liability, state-court proceedings, criminal proceedings or compulsory attendance at a particular place or time. Litigation's political motive or the claim's abstract importance supplies no constitutional immunity rule.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Fitzgerald preserves official presidential decisionmaking immunity; Forrester distinguishes function from identity and Antoine supplies a limited functional analogy. United States v. Nixon rejects absolute insulation from process but reserves civil-evidence disputes. Nixon v. United States, Morgan Stanley, Plaut and Reich retain their confined structural/remedial holdings and establish no general civil exemption.

##### Official-act immunity turns on function, not presidential status

**Holding:** Fitzgerald's absolute personal civil-damages immunity continues to protect conduct within the outer perimeter of presidential official responsibility; title alone cannot convert pre-presidential conduct into an official presidential act, and alleged illegality or improper motive alone does not strip an otherwise official act of that protection. The possible official-function defense concerning post-inauguration press-secretary statements remains for initial District Court consideration on a developed record.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; eight direct rationale votes. Breyer concurs in the judgment only.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The post-inauguration press-secretary statements are not classified on this undeveloped record, and not every pleaded allegation is declared unofficial. No new universal classification sequence, reasonable-officeholder test, motive or grave-wrongdoing exception, or qualified-immunity replacement is adopted; state-office defenses and claim elements remain separate.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Fitzgerald retains its outer-perimeter protection and rejection of improper purpose alone as disqualifying. Forrester distinguishes responsibility performed from present title without diluting that presidential immunity.

##### Concrete presidential duties receive case-specific accommodation

**Holding:** The District Court must give substantial respect to the President's constitutional duties throughout this action; a party seeking protection must identify the particular litigation demand and the conflict or hardship, and any relief must respond to its supported need, duration and alternatives while accounting for the plaintiff's legitimate interests. The court may adjust timing, sequence or manner and may grant a justified, bounded stay, but its discretion cannot authorize significant constitutionally forbidden impairment of another branch's assigned functions.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; eight direct rationale votes. Breyer concurs in the judgment only.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Landis's clear-showing burden applies when even a fair possibility of harm from the requested stay exists. Relief must remain within justified need, duration and alternatives and be reconsidered as circumstances change; neither a term-long presumption nor a burden requiring the plaintiff to prove exceptional/irreparable prejudice is adopted. There is no absolute date/place/method entitlement, universal hearing, special proof quantum, fixed discovery limit or new interlocutory appeal. Concrete compulsion, privilege, sanctions, irreconcilable scheduling conflict and its precise constitutional remedy are reserved.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Proceed under ordinary federal law, determine properly presented official-act and other defenses separately, and assess specifically supported accommodation requests. The denial of immunity and both categorical-stay removals are affirmed unanimously; no liability, damages or immediate fixed-date trial follows. The concurrences supply no additional controlling classification test, scheduling presumption, constitutional exception or attendance standard.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Landis governs a supported stay at its stated harm trigger. Fitzgerald explains the institutional importance of presidential duties without immunizing private conduct. United States v. Nixon's civil-process reservation and Reich's presidential-coercion reservation remain intact.

## Source: [state/holdings/civil-rights.md](../../../state/holdings/civil-rights.md)

#### Sandin v. Conner

**Docket or dockets:** No. 93-1911.
**Decided:** June 19, 1995.

##### Substantive state constraints and meaningful prison liberty

**Holding:** A State creates protected prison liberty when substantive law meaningfully restricts officials’ authority to impose a significant deprivation. Mandatory wording alone is insufficient: courts must examine the genuine constraint in the whole disciplinary scheme and the meaningful restraint, without making state codification necessary for directly protected constitutional liberty.

**Proposition-level authority:** Breyer’s Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Souter and Ginsburg; five Justices adopt this sufficient, nonexclusive state-entitlement rule.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The rule is sufficient and nonexclusive. Mandatory procedures, routine transfers and brief inconveniences do not automatically create entitlements; direct constitutional, unexpected-restraint and sentence-duration interests remain. No atypical-and-significant-hardship test controls.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Hewitt v. Helms: narrows mechanical reliance on mandatory wording while retaining genuine substantive-predicate liberty protection; no wholesale methodology replacement controls.
- Kentucky Department of Corrections v. Thompson: applies distinction between substantive constraints and procedural directions alone.
- Wolff v. McDonnell: preserves significant prison liberty and context-sensitive procedural protection.
- Meachum v. Fano: distinguishes ordinary sentence-authorized management from a genuine protected constraint.
- Vitek v. Jones; Washington v. Harper: preserve distinct directly protected, qualitatively different restraints; this state-entitlement rule is nonexclusive.
- Farmer v. Brennan; Helling v. McKinney: distinguish Eighth Amendment conditions liability from this procedural-liberty inquiry.

##### The reviewed summary judgment and remand

**Holding:** The summary judgment rejecting the reviewed liberty-and-process claim cannot stand on a mechanical reading of Hawaii’s regulatory words or dismissal of genuine punitive constraints. The district court must determine whether the rules genuinely constrain authority, whether the deprivation is meaningful rather than routine, and what process is due; the Court makes no liability or ultimate protected-interest finding.

**Proposition-level authority:** Breyer’s Opinion of the Court and remedial disposition; Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Souter and Ginsburg join the bounded rule-and-remand ground. Four concurring Justices separately would find the interest already established.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Substantial-evidence language is not rewritten as an express acquittal command; the whole misconduct, impartial-adjudication, evidence and sanction scheme matters. Punitive labels alone do not establish liberty; similarity to separately authorized administrative or protective custody alone does not defeat a punitive constraint. Later expungement supplies no retroactive process and discretionary parole consequences are not inevitable sentence extension. Wolff permits refusal of witnesses for irrelevance, lack of necessity or safety or correctional hazards. Neither the four concurring votes finding an established interest nor the dissent's four-vote replacement framework controls under Marks.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Wolff: applies witness qualifications and the separate process inquiry once protected liberty is implicated.
- Hewitt; Thompson: require whole-scheme substantive analysis rather than a one-way mandatory-word inference.
- Meachum: preserves ordinary authorized custody without making every punitive restraint legally unconstrained.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirm the Ninth Circuit in the reviewed respect, leaving reversal of officials' summary judgment effective, and remand for genuine constraint, meaningful deprivation and due process determinations. The Court finds neither ultimate protected liberty nor liability; unrelated amended-complaint claims and defenses are not resolved.

## Source: [state/holdings/civil-rights.md](../../../state/holdings/civil-rights.md)

#### Richardson v. McKnight

**Docket or dockets:** No. 96-318.

**Decided:** 1997-06-23.

##### I. These commercial prison employees are not eligible for Harlow immunity

**Holding:** Guards employed full time by a private, for-profit firm that independently undertakes substantial continuing prison management under limited direct governmental supervision and potential market competition are not entitled to Harlow qualified immunity merely because they exercise delegated governmental custody. Neither the historical showing nor the functional justification establishes that immunity in this institutional setting; brief private assistance, adjunct service, close official supervision and integration into government remain open.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stone-Zsela's opinion of the Court is joined in full by Stevens, O'Connor, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer. Six Justices directly adopt the eligibility rule, its limits and the bounded affirmance/remand.

**Material application:** The historical showing did not establish modern immunity from suit for these employees. Potential competition, contractual accountability and private employment arrangements inform functional need without findings of perfect competition, particular insurance coverage or indemnification.

**Limits and questions not reached:** State action is assumed only for the immunity appeal. Neither a constitutional violation, causation, damages, corporate immunity or liability, nor the existence, elements or burdens of an independent private good-faith defense is decided. Eligibility precedes application of Harlow; no replacement notice or conduct test is adopted.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Denial of qualified immunity is affirmed, 6–3, and the case is remanded for ordinary proceedings on unresolved predicates and independently available defenses. There is no automatic trial entitlement or damages award.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Wyatt's separation of attribution, immunity and a potential merits defense is applied in the commercial custodial setting. Harlow remains for eligible officials. Fletcher requires function-specific justification, while Behrens and Johnson v. Jones distinguish legal immunity review from factual-sufficiency appeals. Elder and Crawford-El preserve the actual governing law and constitutional proof requirements without creating eligibility or an additional evidentiary burden.

## Source: [state/holdings/civil-rights.md](../../../state/holdings/civil-rights.md)

#### Antoine v. Byers & Anderson, Inc., 508 U.S. 429 (1993)

**Docket or dockets:** No. 91-7604.
**Decided:** June 7, 1993.

##### The ministerial transcript function does not carry absolute judicial immunity

**Holding:** Court reporters performing the statutory task of accurately recording or producing a verbatim transcript do not receive absolute judicial immunity merely because their work is important or integral to a judicial proceeding. A claimant must establish the historically and functionally justified immunity; this ministerial task does not involve the discretionary adjudication that supports judicial immunity.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The burden rests on the person claiming immunity. An authorized federal damages action, actionable deprivation, causation, qualified immunity or other defenses and damages remain undecided.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Reverse the absolute-immunity dismissal and remand for the remaining questions through an authorized cause of action; no automatic liability.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Forrester v. White, 484 U.S. 219: functional distinction between adjudication and administration applied to reporting.
- Butz v. Economou, 438 U.S. 478: burden of justifying absolute immunity and functional comparison applied.
- Burns v. Reed, 500 U.S. 478: historical and functional justification required; importance alone is insufficient.
- Imbler v. Pachtman, 424 U.S. 409: immunity for adjudicative functions distinguished from the ministerial transcript task.
- Wyatt v. Cole, May 18, 1992: private-party qualified-immunity holding preserved without extension to the defense decided here.

## Source: [state/holdings/civil-rights.md](../../../state/holdings/civil-rights.md)

#### Buckley v. Fitzsimmons, 509 U.S. 259 (1993)

**Docket or dockets:** No. 91-7849.
**Decided:** June 24, 1993.

##### Investigative fabrication is not protected by absolute prosecutorial immunity

**Holding:** A prosecutor who performs ordinary investigative work to obtain evidence identifying a perpetrator before probable cause has no absolute prosecutorial immunity for alleged fabrication in that function; later courtroom use does not transform the earlier work into advocacy. Genuine evaluation and preparation in an advocate’s role remain protected, and neither all pre-indictment acts nor all post-indictment acts receive a categorical classification.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Thomas (6 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Imbler v. Pachtman, 424 U.S. 409: protected advocacy and genuine preparation preserved; ordinary investigation distinguished.
- Burns v. Reed, 500 U.S. 478: function-specific limits applied to investigation.
- Forrester v. White, 484 U.S. 219, and Antoine, June 7, 1993: function and claimant’s burden control; importance to litigation is insufficient.

**Later-authority backlinks:** Fletcher v. Kalina, June 16, 1997, Ninth Circuit No. 95-36129: the functional distinction extends to a prosecutor’s personal arrest-warrant certification while preserving protected advocacy.

##### A press conference does not carry an advocate’s absolute immunity

**Holding:** A prosecutor’s statements to the press announcing an indictment have no absolute prosecutorial immunity merely because they concern a pending prosecution or cause injury through later judicial proceedings. The ordinary qualified-immunity inquiry and the requirement of an actionable constitutional deprivation remain separate.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, this proposition; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Allegations are not findings. Genuine advocacy preparation and unreviewed in-court presentation immunity remain; paid or coerced witness allegations require initial treatment below. Qualified immunity, constitutional injury, causation and damages are unresolved; defamation alone is not held actionable.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Reverse and remand: no absolute immunity for the pleaded preliminary fabrication (6–3) or press statements (9–0). No liability or witness-immunity change.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Imbler, 424 U.S. 409, and Burns, 500 U.S. 478: press activity lacks the protected advocacy function.
- Tower v. Glover, 467 U.S. 914: policy alone cannot create a new §1983 absolute immunity.
- Harlow v. Fitzgerald, 457 U.S. 800, and Anderson v. Creighton, 483 U.S. 635: separate qualified-immunity law left unchanged.

## Source: [state/holdings/civil-rights.md](../../../state/holdings/civil-rights.md)

#### Fletcher v. Kalina

**Docket or dockets:** Ninth Circuit No. 95-36129; no Supreme Court docket supplied.
**Decided:** June 16, 1997.

##### Protected prosecutorial advocacy remains distinct from personal evidentiary acts

**Holding:** A prosecutor has absolute immunity under §1983 for preparing and filing an information or warrant motion, evaluating evidence for those submissions, and presenting the State's legal case when performing an advocate's function. An allegation of bad purpose or false evidence does not remove that protection from the advocacy function, but the protection does not extend to a distinct personal evidentiary certification merely because the same prosecutor performs both acts.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens's Part I, joined in full by O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: eight Justices. Stone-Zsela joins protection of legitimate advocacy and separation of the personal evidentiary act, but not refusal of an additional dishonesty exception.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The function, rather than a categorical pre-charge or post-charge boundary, determines immunity. No general dishonesty exception to otherwise protected advocacy is adopted.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Imbler preserves charging and courtroom advocacy; Burns separates legal presentation from other acts. Buckley preserves genuine preparation while excluding ordinary investigation; Antoine requires historical and functional justification rather than importance to adjudication.

##### A prosecutor personally certifying arrest-warrant facts acts as a complaining witness

**Holding:** A prosecutor who personally swears to factual assertions offered to establish probable cause for an arrest warrant performs the evidentiary function of a complaining witness and has no absolute prosecutorial immunity for that certification. This classification depends on the personal evidentiary undertaking, not on proof of falsity, and does not withdraw immunity from ordinary courtroom witness testimony.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens's Part II, unanimous.

**Material application:** Kalina personally guaranteed the facts offered to obtain Fletcher's arrest warrant; that evidentiary undertaking is separate from preparing and presenting the charging papers.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Neither actual falsity nor the availability of another truthful affiant determines the functional classification. Ordinary courtroom testimony immunity remains intact.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirm the denial of absolute immunity for the personal certification and remand, while preserving immunity for protected legal preparation, filing and presentation.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Malley supplies the warrant-affiant comparison; official prosecutorial title does not immunize the same evidentiary function. Buckley and Antoine extend their functional distinction to the personal certification, without changing their other boundaries.

##### The function-specific immunity denial leaves constitutional liability and personal defenses unresolved

**Holding:** Denial of absolute immunity for the personal oath leaves Fletcher to establish an actionable constitutional deprivation, the required culpability, materiality, causation and compensable injury; Kalina retains qualified immunity and other available personal defenses. The existing qualified-immunity framework governs the remand, using the law and information relevant to the conduct at issue, without a new defense-burden rule or a finding that any element or defense has been established.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part III of Stevens's opinion, adopted in full by all eight Associate Justices. Stone-Zsela joins the unresolved-liability and bounded-remand propositions and accepts the existing qualified-immunity framework, without joining the source-specific causation and notice explanations.

**Limits and questions not reached:** A judge's warrant does not categorically break causation when material deception procured it, but a corrected truthful account may still establish probable cause. Neither question is resolved here. Disputed historical facts follow ordinary procedural rules; legal immunity is for the judge. No damages judgment or mandatory trial follows.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Hafer preserves personal-capacity liability only on proof of the officer’s own violation and preserves personal defenses. Harlow and Anderson retain objective, particularized clearly-established-law protection. Elder requires consideration of all relevant precedent; Lanier rejects categorical source and factual-twin restrictions, without supplying retrospective notice.

## Source: [state/holdings/civil-rights.md](../../../state/holdings/civil-rights.md)

#### Landgraf v. USI Film Products, 511 U.S. 244

**Docket or dockets:** No. 92-757.

**Decided:** 1994-04-26.

For the other questions in Landgraf v. USI Film Products, 1994-04-26, No. 92-757, see Federal Courts — Determine statutory temporal reach before applying the presumption against new retrospective consequences.

##### Section 102 does not add its new damages to this preenactment conduct

**Holding:** Section 102’s new compensatory and punitive liability does not attach to discriminatory conduct completed before November 21, 1991, because it adds legal consequences without sufficient congressional direction for that reach. Its Title VII compensation supplements, rather than replaces, §706(g) relief and excludes backpay, interest on backpay and other relief authorized by that provision; punitive damages have the distinct statutory predicates of malice or reckless indifference to federally protected rights and exclude government, government-agency and political-subdivision respondents. No such damages award or predicate finding is made here.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens’s §102 application, joined by Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Souter and Ginsburg, controls by five votes. Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas independently reach the same unavailability of new damages through their concurrence. Blackmun disagrees about compensation and reserves punitive damages.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Bennett and Bowen: applied to the new monetary burden attached to completed conduct.
- Bradley: distinguished; the collateral fee award did not create this new compensation or punishment for the underlying private conduct.
- Franklin v. Gwinnett County Public Schools, 503 U.S. 60 (1992): distinguished; appropriate relief in an implied Title IX action does not enlarge Title VII’s earlier express remedial scheme.
- Robertson v. Seattle Audubon Society, 503 U.S. 429 (1992): distinguished; its express substitution of law governing identified pending disputes does not supply the temporal direction missing here.

##### The jury election depends on an available statutory damages claim

**Holding:** Under §102(c), if the complaining party seeks compensatory or punitive damages available under that section, any party may demand a jury trial, and the court must not inform the jury of the statutory monetary limits. Because §102 damages are unavailable for this preenactment conduct, its conditional jury provision supplies no independent right to a new trial; this conclusion does not prohibit applying a genuinely procedural change to an appropriate future procedural act in another case.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens’s jury portion, joined by Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Souter and Ginsburg, supplies the controlling five-Justice explanation. Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas reach the same conditional conclusion in their concurrence; Blackmun would permit a jury election because he would make compensation available.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Kaiser: applied to the enacted condition linking the jury option to §102 damages.
- Bradley: distinguished; current-law treatment of collateral matters does not erase an express condition on a new remedy or automatically reopen a completed trial.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The rule is provision-specific, not a declaration that the whole 1991 Act is inapplicable in pending cases. Section 402(a)'s enactment-date clause, §109(c)'s later-conduct limitation for extraterritorial amendments, and §402(b)'s exception for disparate-impact complaints filed before March 1, 1975 with initial decisions after October 30, 1983 remain distinct. Judicial retroactivity under Harper does not control legislative reach. An express congressional direction concerning the temporal operation of a statute resolves the threshold statutory question, subject to applicable constitutional limits. Such a direction does not alone determine whether Congress may constitutionally require reconsideration of an already completed Article III judgment. The present case concerns statutory consequences for earlier conduct in litigation properly before the courts and does not decide that separate final-judgment issue.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The Fifth Circuit judgment is affirmed, 8–1. No new damages trial, award or remand is ordered, and the existing equitable-remedy judgment and unreviewed constructive-discharge findings remain intact.

## Source: [state/holdings/civil-rights.md](../../../state/holdings/civil-rights.md)

#### Elder v. Holloway, 510 U.S. 510

**Docket or dockets:** No. 92-8579.

**Decided:** 1994-02-23.

##### Appellate review must consider all relevant law on the preserved immunity issue

**Holding:** Whether the asserted federal right was clearly established when the officers acted is a legal question reviewed independently on appeal using all relevant precedent, including decisions neither cited to nor discovered by the district court. That duty does not admit a new claim or factual record, make every considered precedent controlling, or permit later decisions to supply notice retroactively; here the notice inquiry remains fixed at April 27, 1987.

**Proposition-level authority:** Ginsburg's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas. All nine adopt the legal-authority rule and the limited reversal and remand.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court neither grants nor denies ultimate immunity, decides the factual or legal sufficiency of exigency, awards damages, orders new factfinding or an automatic trial, nor imposes a mandatory merits-first sequence. United States v. Al-Azzawy, 784 F.2d 890, is relevant circuit law predating the arrest; its rule concerning a compelled exit and its ultimate exigency disposition must both be evaluated. Depositions about access to guns and five days of advance arrest planning bear in different directions and are not resolved here. Florida warrants and the absence of an Idaho warrant are distinct facts; the Court does not decide that either alone disposes of the home-arrest question. The lower courts must distinguish a coerced exit from a genuinely voluntary appearance under the governing home-arrest doctrine. Ordinary preservation of claims and evidence remains separate from consideration of legal authority on this preserved claim.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Harlow v. Fitzgerald, 457 U.S. 800: applies objective clearly-established-law protection; the defense is preserved, not replaced by a new reliance-only rule.
- Mitchell v. Forsyth, 472 U.S. 511: applies the legal character of the immunity inquiry.
- Pierce v. Underwood, 487 U.S. 552: applies independent appellate review of legal questions; this later decision does not itself supply officers with notice in April 1987.
- Davis v. Scherer, 468 U.S. 183: distinguishes a requirement that the relevant federal right be clearly established from a rule excluding uncited authority; unrelated duties do not defeat immunity.
- Hunter v. Bryant, December 16, 1991: preserves judicial determination under ordinary summary-judgment constraints using the supported nonmovant version of the complete contemporaneous record; its later decision date supplies no retroactive notice.
- Anderson v. Creighton, 483 U.S. 635: preserves the particularized objective inquiry used in current review; because it was decided after April 27, 1987, it is not itself pre-arrest notice authority.
- Payton v. New York, 445 U.S. 573: retains its warrant protection against a nonconsensual home arrest absent exigent circumstances for the lower court's application; this decision does not resolve that application.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The Ninth Circuit is reversed and the case remanded for independent immunity review using all relevant law and the proper summary-judgment record. Ultimate immunity, exigency, Fourth Amendment liability and damages remain undecided; no automatic trial or new factfinding is ordered.

## Source: [state/holdings/civil-rights.md](../../../state/holdings/civil-rights.md)

#### Heck v. Humphrey, 512 U.S. 477

**Docket or dockets:** No. 93-6188.

**Decided:** 1994-06-24.

##### Necessary invalidity on the present in-custody record

**Holding:** A state prisoner in Heck's position, in custody with a lawful opportunity to challenge the conviction, has no presently cognizable §1983 damages claim when success necessarily establishes the invalidity of that outstanding conviction or sentence, unless the judgment has undergone qualifying invalidation. The rule decides that setting; it does not make present habeas availability a universal element or decide plaintiffs who never had a realistic custody-review opportunity or whose custody has ended.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part I of Justice O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. All nine directly adopt this bounded rule; the distinct sufficient rationales are not added together to obtain its support.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Preiser v. Rodriguez, 411 U.S. 475 (1973): its release-channel distinction informs the new, bounded damages prerequisite; its holding is not misdescribed as already deciding damages-only claims.
- Wolff v. McDonnell, 418 U.S. 539 (1974): preserves civil relief compatible with the operative conviction and custody judgment.
- Patsy v. Board of Regents, 457 U.S. 496 (1982): remains the rule against general §1983 exhaustion; this decision addresses present cognizability instead.

##### Statutory coordination supports this limited prerequisite

**Holding:** For the in-custody, conviction-negating damages claim described in Part I, coordination of §1983 with the available habeas channel supports favorable invalidation as a prerequisite, with the common-law malicious-prosecution analogy implementing that coordination. This is a new, limited restriction on §1983, not conversion of the damages complaint into habeas, a general exhaustion rule, or adoption of every malicious-prosecution element.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part II of O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and Souter; five Justices adopt this sufficient statutory explanation at this limited scope. Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg do not join Part II.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Preiser v. Rodriguez: extends statutory coordination to the particular damages claim while preserving the distinct relief and conditions of habeas.
- Wolff v. McDonnell: preserves its permissible civil-remedy category; factual overlap with prosecution does not remove a claim from that category.
- Patsy v. Board of Regents: distinguishes general exhaustion from the newly adopted prerequisite; exhaustion alone cannot satisfy it.
- Carey v. Piphus, 435 U.S. 247 (1978): applies common-law remedial guidance as an aid to construing the federal remedy, not a wholesale importation of a state tort.

##### Qualifying invalidation and deferred accrual

**Holding:** The claim barred under Part I becomes cognizable only when the conviction or sentence has been reversed on direct review, expunged by executive order, declared invalid by a state tribunal authorized to do so, or called into question by a federal court's issuance of a writ of habeas corpus. That claim accrues only upon such invalidation; exhaustion, unsuccessful review, or loss of a review opportunity is not invalidation, and neither immediate accrual with equitable tolling nor a preservation stay is required for this unaccrued claim.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part III of O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; all nine directly adopt the four invalidation routes and the accrual consequence for the barred claim.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Carey v. Piphus: applies its common-law-informed approach to the federal remedy without imposing additional constitutional-liability elements.
- Patsy v. Board of Regents: preserves the distinction between exhaustion and existence of a cognizable damages claim.

##### Independent injuries, claim-specific analysis and this dismissal

**Holding:** A claim whose success does not necessarily establish invalidity falls outside this prerequisite even if it concerns the same investigation or trial; courts must compare the civil claim's elements with the basis of the conviction, and compensatory recovery still requires an actual compensable injury. On the accepted characterization of Heck's presented action, dismissal without prejudice stands on lack of present cognizability, without deciding misconduct, immunity, personal responsibility, preclusion, damages, or entitlement to release.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part IV of O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; all nine directly adopt this boundary, application and remedy.

**Material application:** The lower courts' characterization that Heck's action necessarily attacked the conviction was not disputed in the petition or principal merits brief; the late reply established no separately preserved independent-injury count requiring remand. An unlawful-search claim can remain compatible with a conviction through independent source, inevitable discovery or harmless error, but those possibilities establish neither automatic admissibility of every search claim nor compensation for conviction injury without invalidation.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Wolff v. McDonnell: preserves the distinction between compatible civil relief and relief dependent on invalidating custody.
- Cavanaugh v. Roller, November 30, 1993: distinguishes its opportunity-only claim; the existing §1983 holding remains intact.
- Carey v. Piphus: applies the requirement of actual compensable injury without adjudicating the amount or merits here.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Former prisoners and persons who never had a realistic custody-review opportunity remain outside the question resolved. No universal constitutional malicious-prosecution action, automatic state-law preclusion, automatic habeas conversion or pending-prosecution abstention rule is adopted. Custis's distinct no-meaningful-review reservation in the ACCA channel remains intact. Recognition of a conviction's invalidation by an authorized state tribunal identifies one circumstance in which the present §1983 prerequisite is satisfied. It does not itself create jurisdiction, confer authority to reopen the underlying conviction, or prescribe grounds upon which a state tribunal must grant collateral relief. Those questions remain governed by the independently applicable law authorizing that tribunal's action.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Dismissal without prejudice is affirmed unanimously for lack of present cognizability. The barred claim has not accrued, so its limitations period is not consumed while the prerequisite remains unmet. A later otherwise proper action may follow qualifying invalidation, subject to remaining requirements; no stay, release, automatic conversion to habeas, liability, immunity or damages determination is ordered.

## Source: [state/holdings/civil-rights.md](../../../state/holdings/civil-rights.md)

#### Crawford-El v. Britton

**Docket or dockets:** D.C. Circuit No. 94-7203; no separate Supreme Court docket assigned.

**Decided:** 1997-06-16.

##### Ordinary proof governs unconstitutional motive in this retaliation claim

**Holding:** The ordinary preponderance standard governs proof of unconstitutional motive in this §1983 retaliation claim; neither qualified immunity nor litigation-burden concerns authorize a special clear-and-convincing requirement. Legally competent direct and circumstantial evidence remain available, and all actual constitutional elements and independent defenses must still be established or resolved under their applicable burdens.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stone-Zsela's opinion of the Court, unanimous on all three propositions and their limits. His separate proposed immunity revision is not adopted.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court decides the quantum Kimberlin reserved; it neither abolishes every specificity practice nor resolves Leatherman’s individual-officer pleading reservation beyond the barriers decided here. A motive allegation alone defeats no constitutional element, ordinary procedure or conduct-date immunity defense.

##### Necessary discovery cannot be barred by the rejected heightened proof requirement

**Holding:** A court may resolve a genuinely dispositive legal defense first and require specific, nonconclusory support, but it may not categorically demand proof satisfying the rejected heightened quantum before allowing discovery necessary to obtain that proof. Rule-authorized clarification, replies, relevant and proportionate discovery limits, sequencing, protective orders and ordinary summary-judgment duties remain available; no unrestricted discovery or particular deposition is required.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stone-Zsela's opinion of the Court, unanimous on all three propositions and their limits. His separate proposed immunity revision is not adopted.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The court may clarify allegations, resolve actual dispositive legal defenses and limit discovery to material issues. An adequately supported summary-judgment motion requires responsive evidence; a general credibility attack is insufficient. No fixed discovery plan, particular deposition, unrestricted inquiry, mandatory trial or new interlocutory appeal follows.

##### An actual same-decision showing remains distinct from a conceivable lawful explanation

**Holding:** A merely possible lawful explanation for an official's conduct does not itself establish a same-decision defense or make competent proof of unconstitutional motive inadmissible. Under Mt. Healthy, the claimant must establish protected conduct and its substantial or motivating causal role; the defendant may then prove by a preponderance that the same action would have occurred without that conduct. The separate clearly-established-law defense remains available on the law governing the conduct when it occurred.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stone-Zsela's opinion of the Court, unanimous on all three propositions and their limits. His separate proposed immunity revision is not adopted.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Protected communication, personal responsibility, actual motive, causation, injury and conduct-date immunity remain unresolved. Later Kimberlin and Settle doctrine supplies no notice in 1989. Prison-access limits remain and no unrestricted press-conference entitlement is recognized.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Vacate only the heightened motive burden and dependent directions; preserve the existing remand otherwise. Resolved access-to-courts and due-process claims are not revived. The municipal issue retains its separate panel posture; the reinstated pendent conversion claim remains subject to ordinary jurisdiction and merits. No liability, immunity, damages or trial entitlement is decided.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Kimberlin v. Quinlan: applies the distinction between motive as a constitutional element and objective immunity, competent circumstantial proof and lawful case management; decides here the proof quantum Kimberlin left open.
- Harlow v. Fitzgerald: retains the objective clearly-established-law defense and early legal screening; it supplies no independent heightened evidentiary code.
- Anderson v. Creighton: retains particularized conduct-date notice and appropriately tailored discovery; no ultimate immunity determination is made.
- Leatherman v. Tarrant County: uses its source-based municipal pleading reasoning by analogy while preserving its express individual-officer reservation outside the specific categorical barriers resolved here.
- Siegert v. Gilley: preserves early examination of a cognizable constitutional deprivation without converting its threshold holding into a special motive burden.
- Anderson v. Liberty Lobby: applies a valid governing substantive burden at summary judgment, without authorizing courts to invent a new one.
- New York Times Co. v. Sullivan: distinguishes the constitutional speech basis of its defamation burden from this retaliation action against an official.
- Mt. Healthy City School District Board of Education v. Doyle: applies the claimant's protected-conduct and motivating-factor burden and the defendant's separate preponderance same-decision defense.
- Settle v. Dickson County School Board: applies its actual-causation distinction, not school-specific substantive rules; a possible lawful reason is insufficient by itself.
- Pell v. Procunier and Turner v. Safley: preserve prison-access limitations and supply no unrestricted press-conference right.
- Johnson v. Jones and Behrens v. Pelletier: preserve the distinction between legal immunity review and disputed factual sufficiency; no new interlocutory-review right is created.

## Source: [state/holdings/civil-rights.md](../../../state/holdings/civil-rights.md)

#### Romer v. Evans, 517 U.S. 620

**Docket or dockets:** No. 94-1039.
**Decided:** May 20, 1996.

##### Withdrawal and constitutional foreclosure of ordinary protection for an identified class

**Holding:** Colorado Amendment 2's combined withdrawal of existing orientation-based nondiscrimination protection and foreclosure of comparable protection through ordinary state and local government violates equal protection under ordinary rational-basis review. Its established class-specific disability lacks a rational relationship to the asserted legitimate interests, without reliance on suspect status, political-process strict scrutiny, elimination of generally applicable protection, or a finding about every voter's motive.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Souter), Part I, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Ginsburg and Breyer; seven Justices.

**Material application:** The authoritative state construction covers legislative, executive, and judicial channels of the State, agencies, schools, and local government, including public and private settings; it is not merely a quota prohibition or local preemption leaving the legislature free to act. Uniformity, regulatory restraint, finite resources, religious and intimate association, parental teaching, and conduct-based morality do not explain this blanket ineligibility across public and impersonal commercial life. Ordinary review permits supported predictions, imperfect categories, and conceivable grounds without formal findings or empirical certainty; a narrower alternative alone is insufficient. The defect concerns the relationship to the actual disadvantage, rather than an independent hostility finding or mandatory least-restrictive-means inquiry.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court assumes, without deciding, that generally applicable criminal, tort, contract, and public-service protections remain. It creates no suspect or quasi-suspect tier, general duty to enact nondiscrimination laws, nationwide code, quota entitlement, universal access to every lawmaking level, or presumption invalidating every group-affecting repeal, referendum, or constitutional policy reservation. Particular religious, intimate-association, and expressive defenses retain their own predicates; no municipal exemption is approved.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** *Beach* preserves conceivable legitimate grounds and reasonable generalizations together with the *Moreno/Cleburne* limits on bare harm and irrational prejudice. *Heller*'s controlling proof-burden decision requires a relationship to the actual disadvantage; its separate family-participation holding preserves relevant generalizations and rejects invalidity based on narrower alternatives alone. *Hurley* protects concrete private expression without making ordinary service categorically expressive association; *Swanner* and *American Life League* retain their particular religious-liberty inquiries. *Bowers* remains effective on its specified-conduct question but does not justify this broader disability. *Adarand* adds no orientation tier, and *Klinger*'s treatment-specific comparison does not import *Armstrong*'s charging-discovery threshold into an express classification.

##### Resolution of the preserved alternative and complete injunctive relief

**Holding:** The fully presented rational-basis alternative permits affirmance without adoption of the lower court's strict-scrutiny rationale. The defect permeates Amendment 2's inseparable common operation, warranting the complete existing injunction without a new merits remand or federal rewriting of state law.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part II of the same seven-Justice Court opinion.

**Material application:** State severability law treats orientation, conduct, practices, and relationships as descriptions of the same class; deletion of a description leaves the same disability. No independently lawful remainder is established. Failure under strict scrutiny is not itself rational-basis failure, and no unconditional State concession supplies the result. The ability to seek another constitutional amendment does not cure the unequal barrier to ordinary protection.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirm, 7–2; the permanent injunction remains effective in full. No enactment, damages, factual inquiry, severance proceeding, or new hearing is ordered. Unappealed suspect-status treatment is not reopened, and independently preserved First Amendment claims are neither decided nor presumed abandoned.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** *Hunter* and *Seattle* retain their racial political-restructuring settings without a new fundamental-right extension. *Valtierra*, *Gordon*, and *Crawford* prevent treating every referendum, neutral voting rule, or policy revision as exclusion. *Dalton* requires relief matched to the violation and preservation of independently lawful operation; no general facial formula, *Miller* abortion method, or *44 Liquormart* empirical burden replaces this analysis.

## Source: [state/holdings/civil-rights.md](../../../state/holdings/civil-rights.md)

#### Walmer v. Department of Defense

**Docket or dockets:** Tenth Circuit No. 93-3377.

**Decided:** January 13, 1997.

For the other questions in Walmer v. Department of Defense, Tenth Circuit No. 93-3377, decided January 13, 1997, see Constitutional Structure - Legislative punishment and executive regulation (The attainder prohibition does not extend to this executive military regulation).

##### Ordinary rationality sustains this preliminary denial on the stipulated conduct record

**Holding:** The challenged conduct-based military personnel rule receives ordinary rational-basis review, which permits reasonably conceivable legitimate grounds, reasonable predictions and imperfect fit while requiring a relationship to the actual disadvantage and forbidding bare harm or irrational prejudice. On this preliminary record of admitted homosexual acts and stipulated ineligibility for the retention exceptions, discipline, morale and workable military service relationships supply a permissible predictive relationship, so denial of equal-protection preliminary relief is affirmed without a final ruling on another or materially developed record.

**Proposition-level authority:** O'Connor's Part I, joined by Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Breyer; five Justices.

**Limits and questions not reached:** This is preliminary review of admitted conduct with stipulated exception ineligibility. The full retention-exception criteria, their validity and application to other claimants are not adjudicated. No final validity declaration, orientation-only inference rule, later-statute ruling, Government actual-purpose burden, individualized-impairment requirement or universal scrutiny tier follows. The other equitable factors were assumed below, not found satisfied.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Beach, both relevant Heller propositions and Romer are applied at their actual ordinary-review scope; Goldman and Rostker supply deference without immunity. Bowers, Kelley and Virginia are distinguished at the questions they decided.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirm denial of preliminary equal-protection relief, 5-4; no injunction, final policy-validity declaration or discharge order.

**Later-authority backlinks:** Thomasson v. Perry, Fourth Circuit No. 95-2185, decided June 9, 1997, resolves the reserved statement-based question only for a conduct-related inference with genuine rebuttal under section 654(b)(2); Walmer’s admitted-conduct preliminary holding is unchanged.

## Source: [state/holdings/civil-rights.md](../../../state/holdings/civil-rights.md)

#### Thomasson v. Perry

**Docket or dockets:** Fourth Circuit No. 95-2185; review of 80 F.3d 915 (en banc).

**Decided:** 1997-06-09.

For the speech question in Thomasson v. Perry, Fourth Circuit No. 95-2185, decided June 9, 1997, see First Amendment — Military statements as relevant evidence.

##### A genuinely rebuttable conduct inference sustains this statement-based separation

**Holding:** Ordinary rational-basis review permits the Navy to apply 10 U.S.C. §654(b)(2) to Thomasson's voluntary declaration of homosexual identity as a rebuttable indication of prohibited conduct, propensity, or intent, with propensity meaning a likelihood of acts beyond abstract preference or desire and a genuine opportunity to rebut that inference. On this record, the unrebutted inference rationally relates to legitimate military personnel interests and sustains the discharge without proof of a completed prohibited act or individualized operational impairment; it is not a conclusive disqualification for private orientation.

**Proposition-level authority:** O'Connor's opinion, joined by Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Breyer; five direct joins.

**Material application:** The Board considered service history, supporting accounts and expert evidence and found the inference unrebutted. No exclusion of evidence or compelled denial of identity was established. Service excellence and absence of completed prohibited conduct do not automatically negate the predictive premise.
