Direct majority support at the stated level of generality; no Marks aggregation.
- Protected advocacy and personal warrant certification: Fletcher v. Kalina, Fletcher v. Kalina — I. Protected advocacy and a distinct evidentiary act (eight Associates for the complete advocacy rule), Fletcher v. Kalina — II. Personal factual certification supporting an arrest warrant (all nine), and Fletcher v. Kalina — III. The immunity ruling permits further proceedings, not a liability judgment (eight Associates for the source-specific causation and ordinary qualified-immunity qualifications), 1997-06-16.

**Present operation effective:** Fletcher v. Kalina, 1997-06-16; Buckley's investigation and press components retain their original operation.

**Holdings navigation:** Buckley v. Fitzsimmons, 509 U.S. 259 (1993). Fletcher v. Kalina (1997-06-16) — Protected prosecutorial advocacy remains distinct from personal evidentiary acts; Fletcher v. Kalina (1997-06-16) — Civil Rights / Prosecutorial investigation and press statements.

## Standards and Tests selection: Qualified-immunity legal appeals and factual sufficiency

### Qualified-immunity legal appeals and factual sufficiency

**Governing question:** The boundary of immediate review under §1291 when qualified immunity is denied.

**Current rule:** Immediate review may address the separable legal question whether conduct on the relevant assumed facts violates clearly established law, including the legality of a categorical direct-evidence condition. It may not determine only whether pretrial evidence creates a genuine dispute about the official’s participation, or choose between disputed motive, causation or factual accounts. A legal appeal uses the district court’s assumed facts; if not stated, the appellate court may identify likely assumptions favorable to the nonmovant without deciding truth or reweighing sufficiency. An abstract legal contention does not bring the factual dispute within immediate review. An earlier pleading-stage appeal does not by itself exhaust review of a later summary-judgment order: a separable legal immunity question must independently satisfy the collateral-order conditions. This permits neither repetition of an unchanged ruling nor relitigation of matters resolved by the first mandate. Having to defend a different surviving claim does not itself defeat otherwise authorized claim-specific review, but neither supplies missing authorization nor makes that other claim reviewable.

**Authority by component:**

- Factual sufficiency is not an immediate qualified-immunity appeal: Factual sufficiency is not an immediate qualified-immunity appeal, 1995-06-12. Justice Breyer's opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; all nine adopt this bounded rule.
- Separable legal review of the categorical evidence rule: Separable legal review of the categorical evidence rule, 1995-06-12. Ginsburg's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Breyer; all nine support this legal-review proposition.

- Successive-stage review and preserved mandate limits: Behrens — Holding 1, February 21, 1996; Ginsburg Part I for Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and herself (seven).
- Conditional assumed-fact rule: Behrens — Holding 2, February 21, 1996; unanimous Part II. Claim-specific effect of another surviving claim: Holding 3, Part III for all eight Associates; Stone-Zsela does not join. Stevens and Breyer join these conditional rules while opposing authorization of the successive appeal.

**Present operation effective:** Behrens, February 21, 1996; Johnson and Kimberlin retain their original component authority.

**Open question:** Genuinely necessary connected review and a categorical rule for every mixed order remain unresolved. Johnson and Kimberlin use their common entering baseline; neither is treated as earlier same-day law for the other.

**Holdings navigation:** Factual sufficiency is not an immediate qualified-immunity appeal; Separable legal review of the categorical evidence rule; Behrens v. Pelletier, 516 U.S. 299.

## Standards and Tests selection: Relevant precedent in appellate qualified-immunity review

### Relevant precedent in appellate qualified-immunity review

**Governing question:** What legal sources an appellate court must consider on a preserved clearly-established-law issue.

**Current rule:** Whether a federal right was clearly established when officials acted is reviewed independently using all relevant precedent, including cases neither cited to nor discovered by the district court. This does not admit a new claim or factual record, make each considered precedent controlling or permit later decisions to supply notice retrospectively. The notice inquiry stays fixed to the conduct date. Ordinary claim and evidence preservation, particularized objective immunity and proper summary-judgment treatment remain distinct; no mandatory merits-first sequence follows.

**Authority by component:**

- Independent legal review, contemporaneous notice and preservation limits: Elder v. Holloway — Appellate review must consider all relevant law on the preserved immunity issue, 1994-02-23. Ginsburg's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas. All nine adopt the legal-authority rule and the limited reversal and remand.

**Present operation effective:** Elder v. Holloway, 1994-02-23.

**Holdings navigation:** Appellate review must consider all relevant law on the preserved immunity issue.

## Standards and Tests selection: Public-employee speech and the employer’s mistaken factual account

### Public-employee speech and the employer’s mistaken factual account

**Governing question:** Which employer beliefs about employee speech protect a disciplinary decision, and which do not.

**Current rule:** An employer acting on a genuine, reasonable account of speech that would be disciplinable under the governing public-employee-speech rules is protected even if that factual account is mistaken. Conversely, an unreasonable and incorrect account does not defeat a claim when the employee actually spoke protected speech and that speech caused the adverse action. The employee must show protected speech was a substantial or motivating factor; the employer may establish by a preponderance that it would have taken the same action absent the protected speech. These limited propositions do not impose liability for every investigation defect or adopt the plurality’s complete investigative procedure.

**Authority by component:**

- Seven-Justice genuine-and-reasonable-account safe harbor: Waters v. Churchill — A genuine, reasonable account of disciplinable speech supplies a limited safe harbor, 1994-05-31. O'Connor's plurality opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Souter and Ginsburg, adopts this sufficient condition; Scalia's concurrence in the judgment, joined by Kennedy and Thomas, expressly accepts it under their broader genuine-ground rule. Seven Justices agree at this precise level; only four adopt the full objective-inquiry framework.
- Six-Justice unreasonable-and-false-account limit: Waters v. Churchill — An unreasonable false account cannot defeat actually protected speech, 1994-05-31. O'Connor, Stone-Zsela, Souter and Ginsburg accept this proposition under objective factual care; Stevens's dissent, joined by Blackmun, expressly accepts it under their broader actual-speech rule. Six Justices agree on this proposition, but the two dissenters do not join the reasonable-error safe harbor or the judgment vacating the appellate rule.
- Motive and same-decision proof: Waters v. Churchill — The motive dispute requires remand without restoring defendants' summary judgment, 1994-05-31. O'Connor's four-Justice plurality and Scalia's three-Justice concurrence in the judgment support this common vacatur and limited remand. Blackmun and Stevens would affirm the appellate reversal under their different actual-speech rule; their votes are not counted as support for vacatur.

**Present operation effective:** Waters v. Churchill, 1994-05-31.

**Open question:** Unreasonable belief where the actual speech was unprotected, and a universal investigation checklist, are not resolved. The four-Justice complete reasonableness framework is noncontrolling.

**Holdings navigation:** A genuine, reasonable account of disciplinable speech supplies a limited safe harbor; An unreasonable false account cannot defeat actually protected speech; The motive dispute requires remand without restoring defendants' summary judgment.

## Standards and Tests selection: Prospective restrictions on employees’ citizen expression

### Prospective restrictions on employees’ citizen expression

**Governing question:** Justification of a broad advance restriction on employees’ compensated speech as citizens.

**Current rule:** Under Pickering, the Government must supply a justification commensurate with the actual reach and burden of a broad prospective restriction on employees’ future compensated citizen expression, including the burden on listeners. A general concern about misuse of office does not by itself justify prohibiting protected expression unrelated to employment; the required connection must be adequately established. Narrower valid ethics restrictions remain available.

**Authority by component:**

- A broad prospective honoraria ban requires justification for its actual speech burden: A broad prospective honoraria ban requires justification for its actual speech burden, 1995-02-22. Justice Stevens's Part I, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer, supplies six votes for this rule and explanation. Justice O'Connor agrees with invalidity for unrelated expression on her separate, case-specific Pickering ground; her vote is not counted for this prospective-burden formulation.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-02-22; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** This prospective-burden rule does not constitutionalize every payment or decide materially different senior-employee settings. The existing honoraria ban’s classwide remedy has its own five-Justice statutory-remedial ground; it creates no universal injunction for unrepresented employees or judicially rewritten employment-nexus exception.

**Holdings navigation:** A broad prospective honoraria ban requires justification for its actual speech burden.

## Standards and Tests selection: Military statement-based conduct inference and evidentiary use

### Military statement-based conduct inference and evidentiary use

**Governing question:** The §654(b)(2) statement route, construed as a rebuttable conduct-related military personnel inference.

**Current rule:** Under ordinary rational-basis review, a voluntary declaration of homosexual or bisexual identity may be treated as rebuttable evidence of prohibited acts, propensity or intent where propensity means likelihood of acts beyond abstract preference or desire and a genuine rebuttal opportunity exists. The sustained application requires neither a completed act nor individualized operational impairment; it is not a conclusive orientation exclusion. Section 654(b)(2) directs separation after the approved statement finding unless a further approved finding establishes that the member demonstrated no engagement, attempted engagement, propensity or intent to engage in homosexual acts. The First Amendment permits the relevant evidentiary use at that conduct-related scope, without permitting punishment for abstract belief or opposition to the policy.

**Authority by component:**

- Thomasson v. Perry — I. The conduct-related, rebuttable statement inference is constitutional in this application, 1997-06-09; O'Connor with Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Breyer; five
- Thomasson v. Perry — II. The First Amendment permits this relevant evidentiary use of the statement, 1997-06-09; the same five

**Present operation effective:** Thomasson, 1997-06-09.

**Exceptions and limits:** Section 654(e) permits omission of processing if the military determines both intent to avoid or terminate service and that separation would not serve its best interest; it does not compel omission. The act and marriage routes have distinct predicates and are not adjudicated here. Denial of meaningful rebuttal, an irrelevant inference, an irrebuttable status exclusion or retaliation for advocacy remains distinct. Service excellence is not an automatic defense, and every identity statement is not an admission of an act. No facial validation, civilian-employment rule, new scrutiny tier or investigation checklist follows; independent due-process, APA, directive-validity and substantive-liberty claims remain undecided.

**Holdings navigation:** Thomasson v. Perry (1997-06-09) — A genuinely rebuttable conduct inference sustains this statement-based separation; Thomasson v. Perry (1997-06-09) — First Amendment / Military statements as relevant evidence.

## Standards and Tests selection: Continuing individual controversy and legally unavailable damages

### Continuing individual controversy and legally unavailable damages

**Governing question:** Whether an individual former employee's challenge to an employment rule remains live after departure.

**Current rule:** A prospective individual challenge becomes moot after voluntary departure from covered employment when no intent to return, realistic recurrence or surviving claim is established. A general prayer cannot preserve the controversy by producing nominal damages against a State or an official-capacity defendant whom §1983 does not make a damages person. Consent to payment cannot create the missing cause of action, and certification of an unsettled state-law question cannot supply missing Article III jurisdiction.

**Authority by component:**

- Arizonans for Official English v. Arizona — A continuing personal controversy is absent, 1997-03-03; Stevens for all nine supplies the continuing-controversy and unavailable-damages limits
- Arizonans for Official English v. Arizona — Certification cannot restore the controversy, 1997-03-03; Stevens for all nine supplies the certification boundary

**Present operation effective:** Arizonans for Official English, 1997-03-03.

**Exceptions and limits:** This is no categorical express-pleading requirement for nominal damages and does not decide a legally available personal-capacity damages claim. Limited authority to correct lower-court jurisdictional error is distinct from merits authority; judgment vacatur remains equitable under the existing rule. No automatic initiative-sponsor standing or mandatory certification rule is established.

**Holdings navigation:** Arizonans for Official English v. Arizona (1997-03-03) — Federal Courts / Mootness, corrective jurisdiction and equitable vacatur.

## Standards and Tests selection: Habeas custody in a continuous consecutive sentence series

### Habeas custody in a continuous consecutive sentence series

**Governing question:** Custody under an earlier conviction after its first consecutive segment has been served.

**Current rule:** A prisoner serving a continuous, unexpired series of consecutive sentences remains in custody under an earlier conviction when invalidating it would advance release from the aggregate term, even though that first segment has been served. This establishes only the custody threshold; procedural gates and the conviction’s merits remain separate.

**Authority by component:**

- Custody under a continuous consecutive series: Custody under a continuous consecutive series, 1995-05-30. Ginsburg’s Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Breyer; eight Justices directly support this rule.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-05-30; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** Maleng’s rule for an independently expired sentence survives. Collateral consequences after all custody ends do not supply jurisdiction through this rule.

**Holdings navigation:** Custody under a continuous consecutive series.

## Standards and Tests selection: Conviction-negating damages claims and favorable invalidation

### Conviction-negating damages claims and favorable invalidation

**Governing question:** When a prisoner’s §1983 damages claim must await invalidation of the conviction or sentence.

**Current rule:** For an in-custody claimant with a real lawful opportunity to challenge the conviction, a §1983 damages claim whose success necessarily establishes the invalidity of that conviction or sentence cannot proceed before favorable invalidation. Qualifying invalidation is reversal on direct appeal, executive expungement, a declaration of invalidity by an authorized state tribunal, or a federal writ of habeas corpus calling it into question. The court compares the actual civil theory and criminal basis, not labels. Independent injuries whose success is compatible with the criminal judgment remain separately cognizable. The barred claim accrues upon qualifying invalidation; the rule is not a direction to file now and seek a stay or an invented tolling regime. Within the in-custody category, damages or retrospective declarations alleging dishonest concealment of exculpatory evidence or disabling adjudicator bias in an outstanding earned-credit deprivation likewise require invalidation when success necessarily defeats that adjudication. Classify each alleged procedural injury by its legal consequence: a genuinely independent failure to state supporting facts and compatible prospective relief may proceed under ordinary requirements. An invalidity-dependent claim is dismissed without prejudice, not stayed for exhaustion; failed review or loss of a review opportunity is not qualifying invalidation.

**Authority by component:**

- Limited in-custody necessary-invalidity prerequisite: Heck v. Humphrey — I. Necessary invalidity on the present in-custody record; II. Statutory coordination supports this limited prerequisite, 1994-06-24. Part I of Justice O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. All nine directly adopt this bounded rule; the distinct sufficient rationales are not added together to obtain its support. Part II of O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens and Souter; five Justices adopt this sufficient statutory explanation at this limited scope. Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg do not join Part II.
- Qualifying invalidation, accrual and independent injuries: Heck v. Humphrey — III. Qualifying invalidation and deferred accrual; IV. Independent injuries, claim-specific analysis and this dismissal, 1994-06-24. Part III of O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; all nine directly adopt the four invalidation routes and the accrual consequence for the barred claim. Part IV of O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Blackmun, Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; all nine directly adopt this boundary, application and remedy.

- Edwards v. Balisok — I. Procedural labels do not avoid necessary invalidity, 1997-05-19; Scalia, all nine
- Edwards v. Balisok — II. Genuinely independent procedural injuries require their own analysis, 1997-05-19; all nine
- Edwards v. Balisok — III. Qualifying invalidation precedes accrual; an exhaustion stay is not the remedy, 1997-05-19; all nine
- Edwards v. Balisok — IV. Prospective date stamping remains for ordinary equitable adjudication, 1997-05-19; all nine

**Present operation effective:** Edwards v. Balisok, 1997-05-19, extending the carried Heck rule to invalidity-dependent disciplinary-credit claims.

**Open question:** Two distinct populations remain unresolved: former prisoners no longer in custody, and persons who never had a realistic opportunity for habeas review regardless of custody status. Neither reservation is an exemption already granted. Patsy’s ordinary no-exhaustion rule and Cavanaugh’s opportunity-only prospective claim remain effective.

**Holdings navigation:** Necessary invalidity on the present in-custody record; Statutory coordination supports this limited prerequisite; Qualifying invalidation and deferred accrual; Independent injuries, claim-specific analysis and this dismissal. Edwards v. Balisok (1997-05-19) — Procedural labels do not avoid necessary invalidity; Edwards v. Balisok (1997-05-19) — Genuinely independent procedural injuries require their own analysis; Edwards v. Balisok (1997-05-19) — Qualifying invalidation precedes accrual; an exhaustion stay is not the remedy; Edwards v. Balisok (1997-05-19) — Prospective date stamping remains for ordinary equitable adjudication.

## Standards and Tests selection: Ministerial court reporting and absolute judicial immunity

### Ministerial court reporting and absolute judicial immunity

**Governing question:** What instructions govern ministerial court reporting and absolute judicial immunity?

**Current rule:** Accurate recording and production of a verbatim transcript are ministerial functions and do not carry absolute judicial immunity merely because they are important or integral to adjudication. The claimant of immunity must establish its historical and functional justification; discretionary adjudication supplies a different function. Denial of absolute immunity establishes neither a cause of action nor liability, and other defenses remain open.

**Authority by component:**

- The ministerial transcript function does not carry absolute judicial immunity: Antoine v. Byers & Anderson, Inc. — The ministerial transcript function does not carry absolute judicial immunity, 1993-06-07. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court; Stone-Zsela, White, Blackmun, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas (9 Justices). Direct majority support at this level of generality; no Marks synthesis.

**Present operation effective:** 1993-06-07; Antoine v. Byers & Anderson, Inc., for the components identified above.

**Holdings navigation:** Antoine v. Byers & Anderson, Inc., 508 U.S. 429 (1993).

## Standards and Tests selection: Qualified-immunity eligibility of commercial prison employees

### Qualified-immunity eligibility of commercial prison employees

**Governing question:** Whether employees of an independently managed commercial prison receive Harlow immunity for delegated custody.

**Current rule:** Full-time guards of a private for-profit firm independently undertaking substantial continuing prison management, under limited direct governmental supervision and potential market competition, do not receive Harlow qualified immunity merely because they perform governmental custody. State attribution and immunity eligibility are separate; eligibility requires its own historical and functional justification before the content of qualified immunity is applied.

**Authority by component:**
- Institutional eligibility exclusion: Richardson v. McKnight, Richardson v. McKnight — I. These commercial prison employees are not eligible for Harlow immunity, Stone-Zsela with Stevens, O'Connor, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer (six), 1997-06-23.
- Unresolved merits and private defense: Richardson, Richardson v. McKnight — II. Denial of immunity does not establish a constitutional violation or personal liability, same six-Justice opinion, 1997-06-23.

**Present operation effective:** Richardson v. McKnight, 1997-06-23.

**Open question:** Brief private assistance, adjunct service, close official supervision and governmental integration remain open. So do the availability, elements and burdens of a separate private good-faith defense. The rule establishes neither state action, constitutional injury, causation, damages nor corporate liability, and leaves eligible public officials' Harlow protection intact.

**Holdings navigation:** Richardson v. McKnight (1997-06-23) — I. These commercial prison employees are not eligible for Harlow immunity; Richardson v. McKnight (1997-06-23) — Civil Rights / Section 1983 immunity.

## Standards and Tests selection: Presidential civil immunity and case-specific litigation accommodation

### Presidential civil immunity and case-specific litigation accommodation

**Governing question:** A federal damages action for a sitting President's alleged unofficial conduct before office.

**Current rule:** Presidential status supplies no temporary constitutional immunity requiring dismissal or suspension of the federal action through the term. Fitzgerald's absolute damages immunity still protects acts within the outer perimeter of official presidential responsibility; title cannot make pre-office conduct official, while asserted illegality or improper motive does not alone remove official protection. Courts must give substantial respect to presidential constitutional duties. A party seeking protection must identify the particular litigation demand and conflict or hardship; relief must fit supported need, duration and alternatives while accounting for the plaintiff's timely adjudication and evidence interests. When even a fair possibility exists that a requested stay will harm the other party, the applicant must make a clear showing of hardship or inequity. Timing, sequence, manner or a justified bounded stay may be adjusted, but generalized burdens and the term's duration do not justify a categorical stay, and discretion cannot authorize significant constitutionally forbidden impairment of another branch's assigned functions.

**Authority by component:**

- Clinton v. Jones — II. No temporary immunity for the presented pre-presidential conduct, 1997-05-27; Scalia with Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; eight
- Clinton v. Jones — III. Official-act immunity turns on function, not presidential status, 1997-05-27; the same eight
- Clinton v. Jones — IV. This categorical trial stay is an abuse of discretion, 1997-05-27; the same eight
- Clinton v. Jones — V. Concrete presidential duties receive case-specific accommodation, 1997-05-27; the same eight

- Integrated appellate scope: Clinton v. Jones — I. Immediate review reaches this integrated immunity and trial-protection order, 1997-05-27; the same eight

**Present operation effective:** Clinton v. Jones, 1997-05-27.

**Exceptions and limits:** No term-long presumption, shifted burden or concurrence-derived classification test follows. State-court proceedings, criminal liability, concrete compulsion and an actual irreconcilable conflict remain unresolved. A conclusively rejected immunity claim permits review of the same order's integrated categorical trial protection and alternative docket-control ground, not independent liability questions or automatic appeal of later scheduling orders.

**Holdings navigation:** Clinton v. Jones, 520 U.S. 681 (1997) (1997-05-27) — No temporary immunity for the presented pre-presidential conduct; Clinton v. Jones, 520 U.S. 681 (1997) (1997-05-27) — Official-act immunity turns on function, not presidential status; Clinton v. Jones, 520 U.S. 681 (1997) (1997-05-27) — This categorical trial stay is an abuse of discretion; Clinton v. Jones, 520 U.S. 681 (1997) (1997-05-27) — Concrete presidential duties receive case-specific accommodation; Clinton v. Jones, 520 U.S. 681 (1997) (1997-05-27) — Immediate review reaches this integrated immunity and trial-protection order.

**Opinion-assignment seniority:** For each judgment component, the Chief Justice assigns when he is in the judgment majority. Otherwise the most senior participating Associate Justice in that majority assigns, in this order: Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg, Breyer.

## Standing State selection: 3. Standing Practices

## 3. Standing Practices

**Referral of applications, effective October 7, 1991; signed explanations and certification, effective October 2, 1995.** By the Court's internal practice adopted at the opening of October Term 1991, an application for a stay, injunction, or other interim relief in a matter on the Term's inventory is referred by the Circuit Justice to the full Court and decided by the participating Justices under the participating-majority rule. The order issues in the Court's name and recites the presenting Circuit Justice ("presented to Justice ___ and by him or her referred to the Court"). From October 2, 1995, referral does not prevent the Circuit Justice, or the substitute the term-effective Rules designate, from taking a necessary temporary action in his or her own name while the Court considers the application. Each action is explained in short signed writings, including the Circuit Justice's own explanation at final disposition, whether prevailing or dissenting, and a statement by each participating Justice in each Court action deciding relief, and is certified for issuance by the Chief Justice or the Chief's substitute, as Engine §10 provides. A Justice may participate in a referred application from the date of the judicial oath. An application not on the Term's inventory remains subject to ordinary Circuit Justice practice. Source: the OT1991 opening practice carried in the opening Standing State and the OT1993 Term Continuity Note. Source of the October 2, 1995 revision: user instruction.

## Current-term public-law selections

Source: [Current-Term Neutral Projection](../workspace/neutral-projection.md); linked Public Projections identify authority. Copied verbatim from the authorized sanitized projection; no raw Record was read.

### October 20, 1997 — Brown v. Williams

[Public Projection](../records/brown_v_williams_filing_order_1997-10-20.md#public-projection). The per curiam Court applies existing filing-access law. Rule 39 financial eligibility and Rule 39.8 screening remain separate: denial requires a supported finding that the particular submission is frivolous or malicious. Eight prior unsuccessful petitions and an earlier denial do not establish that predicate for the present paper. Access allegations require examination but create no privileged claim category; none is found true. A prospective noncriminal-certiorari fee-and-form condition independently requires demonstrated applicant-specific abuse, fit to that category, and support for the inadequacy of individual-paper treatment. Keeping paid and criminal channels open limits a condition's reach but does not justify it.

Martin, Demos, In re Vey, Vey v. Clinton, Zatko, Day, Sassower, and the applicant-specific cases retain their distinct scopes. McDonald and Sindram's extraordinary-writ authority does not automatically justify controls over another category. No numerical threshold, universal warning requirement, exhaustion of every conceivable alternative, new presumption, or categorical prohibition on supported restrictions is created. Financial leave permits processing only: no certiorari ruling, access violation, entitlement to briefing or counsel, reversal of the Sixth Circuit, or relief from lower-court default follows. No payment deadline, refiling obligation, or future restriction is imposed.

### November 17, 1997 — Jeffries v. Harleston

[Public Projection](../records/jeffries_v_harleston_merits_1997-11-17.md#public-projection). Souter's Parts I and III command five direct votes (Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg, Breyer); Part II commands those five plus Stone-Zsela. Six Justices support vacatur; the controlling five-vote mandate does not require a new trial.

Part I rejects final defense judgment on the panel's employment balance. Pickering and Connick require attention to the actual speech, service relationship, and institutional needs. Retained tenure and lack of entitlement to a longer chair term do not categorically defeat retaliation; a reasonable forecast matters but does not alone decide protection. The public-concern address was off campus at an unaffiliated event and was not assigned or controlled as official duty; introduction by the chair title did not establish sponsorship. Real administrative duties and recruitment, fundraising, faculty-interaction, and community interests remain relevant, but no special policymaking, confidential, or additional public-contact role was established. Both jury findings—no actual disruption and reasonable expected disruption—stand. Prevention need not await actual harm. Waters protects a genuinely relied-upon reasonable account only if it describes legally disciplinable speech; no complete investigation code is adopted. Perry, Umbehr, and O'Hare preserve the distinction between benefit entitlement and retaliation. No categorical protection of the entire address, actual-loss requirement, quantified institutional-damages requirement, or wholesale NTEU prospective-ban burden follows; Settle remains confined to its student-curriculum setting.

Part II requires each defendant's actual causal responsibility and a real counterfactual under Mt. Healthy, Umbehr, O'Hare, Kimberlin, and Crawford-El. Once protected speech is a substantial or motivating cause, the same-decision defense requires proof by a preponderance, not a conceivable lawful reason. Nine successful one-year votes and differentiated animus or punitive findings do not automatically establish an independent lawful Board decision. A causally effective proposal may matter, but procurement alone proves no liability; nonvoting proposals, defeated votes, and abstention neither establish nor eliminate other supported causal contributions. Competent direct and circumstantial proof applies without a heightened motive burden. A genuine independent lawful decision or supported personal defense may still dispose of a claim; Bernstein's discontinued claims stay discontinued.

Part III leaves actual verdict harmonization and consequences, sustainable individual defenses, capacity attribution, and relief to ordinary adjudication. Harlow and Anderson v. Creighton require personal-damages immunity at the late-October 1991 conduct date and, for any preserved March 1992 claim, its distinct date; later law supplies no retrospective notice. Punitive answers do not replace protection, causation, or immunity. Prospective relief requires a live remedy; elapsed time alone proves neither mootness nor continuing eligibility. Vacatur reinstates neither the two-year chair injunction nor punitive awards and establishes no final liability, immunity, damages, chair entitlement, new March 1992 claim, or universal retrial requirement. Each controlling part has a direct majority; no combined plurality or Marks rule is necessary.

### November 24, 1997 — United States v. Rybar

[Public Projection](../records/united_states_v_rybar_merits_1997-11-24.md#public-projection). Kennedy's Parts I–II command all nine votes; Part III commands eight, with Stone-Zsela dissenting from remand. Part I applies Hale's anti-evasion/material-impairment rule to uphold §922(o)'s possession component as a necessary part of Congress's direct prohibition on additions to the post-1986 civilian machinegun market; Rybar adds no new Commerce doctrine and does not enlarge Hale. The two completed submachine guns and consecutive gun-show sales make the market relation concrete and place these possessions at the core of that mechanism, although the convictions are for possession. There is no transaction-specific interstate-crossing element. Under Lopez's controlling sequence the regulated class and interstate mechanism come first; marketability, remote interstate history, generalized gun violence, or presumed foreign or military provenance alone is not the ground. No universal legislative-findings requirement or general police power follows. Government channels and lawful pre-May 19, 1986 possession retain their statutory scope; manufacture date alone proves no exception.

Part II permits personal invocation of the Second Amendment without militia enrollment or service. Military usefulness neither excludes an arm nor establishes immunity; commerce does not categorically erase the right, and necessary acquisition connects to protected keeping. Love, Hale, and Miller require separate attention to the actual weapons, conduct, and restriction. Commerce authority and registration compliance do not resolve that inquiry; Staples supplies statutory culpability, not a constitutional weapon classification.

Part III vacates the affirmance of Counts I and III because the militia prerequisite was material and the lower court's additional discussion was not an adequate independent weapon-and-restriction application. The known automatic characteristics strengthen the regulation argument but do not alone resolve the possession restriction. Hale's registration holding does not validate this prohibition. Existing evidence or genuinely necessary ordinary proceedings may supply the application; no empirical study, automatic hearing, new scrutiny tier, comprehensive catalogue, categorical automatic-weapons exclusion, general danger exception, or universal remand rule is required. Ultimate constitutional validity remains open. The Court orders no acquittal, release, weapon return, trial, or resentencing. Dismissed NFA transfer Counts II and IV stay outside the judgment; receivers, conversion parts, accessories, ordinary semiautomatic arms, and every other §922(o) application are not resolved.

### December 1, 1997 — United States v. Parker

[Public Projection](../records/united_states_v_parker_merits_1997-12-01.md#public-projection). O'Connor's unanimous opinion applies Mussari/Schroeder to the original §228: Congress may punish willful nonpayment of a valid, legally fixed support obligation between a Florida obligor and children in Pennsylvania. Different-state residence is essential; nonpayment longer than one year or greater than $5,000 is an alternative threshold, not a substitute for the interstate relation. No completed payment, travel, flight, wire, voluntary crossing, or evasion motive is added. The required cross-border payment, rather than attenuated poverty or welfare costs, supplies the commerce ground under Lopez.

The law regulates the private obligor and does not commandeer a State. New York and Printz/Mack retain their independent constraints; absence of commandeering alone creates no commerce power. Federal prosecution accepts the valid state order and authorizes no creation or revision of support, custody, parentage, or domestic status, or disregard of a lawful state modification. Proper challenges to the predicate's existence and continuing force remain available. Only willful failures after October 25, 1992 constitute the offense; earlier arrears may establish background or past-due status. Every actual element requires proof beyond reasonable doubt, with lawful ability-related and other defenses preserved. Rybar changes neither the direct-payment rule nor its limits. Affirmance reinstates prosecution only; guilt, sentence, restitution amount, and treatment of older arrears in any later restitution order remain undecided.

### December 8, 1997 — Richenberg v. Cohen

[Public Projection](../records/richenberg_v_cohen_merits_1997-12-08.md#public-projection). O'Connor's five-Justice opinion, joined by Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, and Breyer, applies Thomasson's ordinary-rationality standard, conduct-related likelihood beyond abstract desire, and genuine individualized rebuttal requirement to sustain this discharge. Meaningful rebuttal does not require acceptance of every denial, proof of completed prohibited acts, or individual operational injury. Service and character evidence must be actually considered but does not automatically defeat the inference. Two hearings considered that evidence and Richenberg's denial; equivocal answers about future conduct permit the existing conduct-related assessment. Discounting general character testimony may reflect its probative reach rather than refusal to consider it.

Attraction alone remains insufficient and ordinary attraction is not a statutory act. Sparse reasons or a competing inference alone establish neither a constitutional violation nor a general requirement of additional findings. The supported reading requires no invented demeanor finding or additional agency reason; hypothetical appellate reasons cannot replace actual reasons. Section 654(b)(2) permits rebuttal by showing no engagement, attempt, propensity, or intent concerning homosexual acts. Section 654(e)'s permission to omit processing requires avoidance-or-termination intent and military-best-interest findings, neither established here, and creates no retention command.

Walmer's preliminary admitted-conduct case does not decide this statement challenge. Romer preserves ordinary rationality, reasonable prediction, and conceivable legitimate grounds tied to the actual disadvantage; Weiss gives military judgment substantial weight while retaining independent constitutional review. Jeffries's actual-ground requirement survives, but its civilian balance and retaliation same-decision rule do not replace Thomasson. No heightened scrutiny, actual-purpose burden, attraction-alone rule, or universal administrative checklist is adopted. Independent APA reviewability and relief, speech, due process, directive validity, substantive liberty, and other ungranted claims remain unresolved. The discharge stands; no new hearing, reinstatement, or compensation is ordered.

### December 15, 1997 — City of Chicago v. International College of Surgeons

[Public Projection](../records/city_of_chicago_v_international_college_of_surgeons_merits_1997-12-15.md#public-projection). Thomas's Part I commands seven votes: Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, and Breyer. Part II's appellate mandate has those six Associate Justices; Stone-Zsela joins reversal with a separate district-court instruction. Stevens and Ginsburg would affirm the jurisdictional remand.

Substantial federal constitutional claims on these complaints' face supply §1331 jurisdiction and §1441(a) removal. Section 1367(a) reaches their related state constitutional and record-confined administrative-review claims because the property designations, permit denials, procedures, injury, and relief form the same Article III case. Gibbs informs the common-case relation; supplemental claims need no independent original-jurisdiction basis. Illinois's closed-record review and presumption favoring agency facts define the task rather than exclude it categorically from §1367. State review standards remain intact. These local administrative decisions are not state-court judgments; an action containing only a state administrative appeal and a diversity basis for one remain undecided.

Power does not compel retention. Section 1367(c) permits declining where state-law issues are novel or complex, state claims substantially predominate, all original-jurisdiction claims are dismissed, or exceptional circumstances present other compelling reasons. Economy, convenience, fairness, and comity inform discretion without replacing those conditions. Abstention retains independent predicates. Burford requires timely and adequate state review and either difficult state-law questions of transcendent public importance or disruption of coherent state policy on a matter of substantial public concern. Quackenbush limits outright Burford surrender to equitable or otherwise discretionary relief; an independent legal-damages claim may be stayed on a justified showing, but not simply relinquished on that ground. No abstention application is decided.

Part II returns to the Seventh Circuit for properly remaining retention and abstention arguments, without restoring the District Court's entire merits judgment. Peacock's later new-debtor action lacked an original federal anchor; its rule survives. Things Remembered's covered-remand review bar is unchanged because this review concerns an appellate judgment following a district merits judgment. Steel Co. retains jurisdiction-first analysis without adding power. No constitutional validity, taking, hardship, sufficiency, demolition entitlement, compulsory retention, categorical administrative-appeal jurisdiction, §1447(d) exception, or alteration of state standards follows. Lakefront's separate action remains outside the judgment. Original complaints, agency materials, and briefs are not reproduced; an asserted lack of state precedent remains a litigant contention rather than an exhaustive finding.

### December 29, 1997 — Clanton v. Cooper

[Public Projection](../records/clanton_v_cooper_merits_1997-12-29.md#public-projection). Ginsburg's Part I and Part II-B are unanimous. Part II-A's complete knowing-or-reckless material-warrant-falsity rule has all eight Associate Justices; Stone-Zsela joins its deliberate-falsity application and subsidiary-theory limits. Part III's distinct confession remand has the eight Associate Justices; Stone-Zsela would affirm denial on that branch. Legal immunity rejection for the assumed deliberate warrant and later detention deception, dismissal of factual-appeal portions, and preservation of rejected theories and disposed claims are unanimous; no liability finding follows.

Johnson and Behrens permit interlocutory immunity review of separable legal defenses on properly assumed plaintiff-favorable historical facts, not participation, credibility, or factual sufficiency. Section 1254 does not enlarge the circuit's §1291 authority. Miller makes ultimate constitutional voluntariness distinct from historical facts; Ornelas supplies task-specific allocation rather than a universal mixed-question rule. Calling an entire coercion inquiry factual cannot exclude a real legal defense, and legal labels cannot secure factual review. The unavailable factual portions must be dismissed.

Franks and Malley supplied the pre-August-1993 rule against personally responsible knowing or reckless falsehoods material to probable cause; judicial issuance does not categorically insulate the officer. Culpable falsity, materiality, and responsibility remain necessary: a corrected truthful account may establish probable cause, and later recantation alone does not prove fabrication. The Court does not find affidavit contents or treat Bobby's derivative statement as independent probable cause. Sufficiently reliable hearsay remains usable; filed charges are not universally necessary before a warrant; and the federal grand-jury requirement does not govern state prosecutions. These subsidiary limits supply neither universal accomplice reliability nor federal liability for every state-law defect.

The same material-deception law obviously applies to the assumed personal communication of deliberate homicide and no-bond falsehoods actually causing otherwise unjustified additional unreasonable physical detention. A different custodian and electronic transmission require no identical NCIC precedent. The original arrest and later message's asserted detention extension remain distinct. The $5,000 Oklahoma bond establishes no federal release entitlement; Texas bond-setting authority establishes no inevitable independent detention. Falsehood, culpability, materiality, attributable cause, and an actual unreasonable seizure must be proved. Inaccuracy, bad motive, a state-law breach, or one-to-three days alone is insufficient. The same-lawful-detention and no-causal-role defenses survive, and no universal duration of Fourth Amendment coverage is decided.

Part III requires legal review of the distinct involuntary-third-party-statement theory on assumed historical facts, identifying Clanton's own protected interest, the particular use, legal voluntariness, and clear establishment at the relevant 1993 conduct date. The lower court discussed her own interest and notice; its error was the overbroad factual characterization, not complete omission. A statement involuntary but not knowingly false presents a distinct theory. Michael's personal self-incrimination privilege is not borrowed, but that does not categorically exclude Clanton's independent liberty injury. Albright supplies no controlling synthesis, universal Fourth Amendment displacement, or general state-remedy bar.

Jackson, Blackburn, Spano, Haynes, and Fulminante supply voluntariness background; Frazier makes an evidence lie nonconclusive, and Hutto does not make every promised benefit coercive. Threats, leniency, suggested narrative, and asserted physical evidence require the applicable totality inquiry. LaFrance, Merkt, Chiavola, Bradford, and relevant circuit law must be evaluated for their actual reach; third-party trial-use holdings do not automatically establish a particular August 1993 pretrial-detention damages right. Obvious coverage needs no identical case, but an abstract liberty principle or bad intent cannot establish an unsettled extension. Ultimate coercion, the independent due-process right, notice, and immunity on that branch remain open.

Harlow/Anderson remains the objective, particularized conduct-date defense. Elder requires all relevant pre-existing law; Lanier supplies a current clarity analogy without retroactive notice or merger of civil and criminal elements. No mandatory merits-first sequence precludes a dispositive notice ground. Crawford-El and Jeffries preserve ordinary competent proof, actual elements, personal responsibility, and causation without a heightened motive burden; their retaliation burden allocation is not universal fabrication law. Fletcher's function distinction supplies neither prosecutorial immunity to Cooper nor 1993 notice. The eight-Associate opinion adopts these limits; Stone-Zsela joins their deliberate-deception application and proposes a noncontrolling replacement defense below.

The existing district immunity denial remains subject to lawful claim-specific review. No new contextual defense, burden shift, general fabrication or pretrial due-process right, derivative Fifth Amendment right, automatic discovery, trial, hearing, or damages follows. The lower opinion supplies assumed facts; original affidavit, full transcript, pleadings, depositions, messages, and later state voluntariness ruling are not reproduced. Its release-year discrepancy and one-versus-three-day ambiguity supply no exact date or amount. Michael's plea and later ruling do not bind Clanton absent established preclusion predicates, and missing materials prove no litigant default.

### January 5, 1998 — Valot v. Southeast Local School District Board of Education

[Public Projection](../records/valot_v_southeast_local_school_district_board_of_education_merits_1998-01-05.md#public-projection). Souter's Parts I and IV have Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg, and Breyer; Part II has all eight Associate Justices, with Stone-Zsela agreeing in its judgments; Part III has Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg, and Breyer. The count-1 categorical rejection is reversed and corresponding defense summary judgment vacated, 6–3. Count 2's protected administrative invocation, count 3's protected-right penalty branch, and count 5's state-claim nonretention return, 5–4. Rejection of count 3's independent economic equality theory and count 4's substantive due process is affirmed, 9–0. No procedural-due-process or renewal-property claim is revived.

A public employer may not deny continued employment solely because an employee lawfully and non-shamly uses this established governmental unemployment-benefit mechanism and imposes its prescribed benefit cost; public-concern content is unnecessary for this particular invocation. Perry separates employment entitlement from retaliation protection; California Motor Transport protects governmental redress, while Smith v. Arkansas State Highway Employees distinguishes protection from retaliation from an obligation to accept demands. Connick, Pickering, and Rankin retain employee-speech rules, but this external formal proceeding is not merely an informal workplace complaint. Neither every personal request or internal grievance nor a universal success condition is decided.

The Board admitted that applications and receipts caused nonrenewal. Its dollar-for-dollar cost was real but was the lawful expense of the very invocation punished; no separately established disruption or fraud justifies that action. Later reasonable-assurance practices prove neither summer 1993 assurance nor ineligibility. Eligibility contests, rejection of fraudulent or ineligible claims, neutral filing conditions, lawful prospective changes, and independent job-related decisions remain available. The program is not frozen and operational costs are not universally irrelevant.

Part II holds that known benefit applications rationally indicate possible recurring fiscal expense when considered independently of a protected-right penalty. Beach, Heller, and Romer retain their actual ordinary-equality limits; that conceivable fiscal basis establishes neither a valid rights penalty nor a Mt. Healthy same-decision defense. Collins does not convert voluntary employment or state-law expectations into a distinct fundamental renewal right or substantive deprivation. Annual contracts and an anticipated recommendation guaranteed no renewal; plaintiffs' express substantive-only limitation leaves procedural due process outside the case. Albright remains fractured, without a universal specific-source or state-remedy bar.

Part III returns count 2 because successful receipt does not foreclose retaliation for using the process; it creates no freestanding general access entitlement. The rights-dependent equality branch returns on the corrected protection premise without automatic strict scrutiny or independent liability. The Ohio claim's nonretention must be reconsidered because federal claims survive. Section 1367(c)(3) permits rather than compels declining after all original claims are dismissed and does not eliminate supplemental power; Chicago's power/discretion distinction governs. The state merits, compulsory retention, and other supported grounds for nonretention remain open.

Parts I and IV require ordinary proof of actual causation, personal responsibility, municipal attribution, injury, immunity, and relief. The Board's reason establishes neither each individual's participation nor all remedial predicates; the superintendent's favorable recommendation is not an adverse vote. Under Mt. Healthy, Umbehr, O'Hare, and Crawford-El, protected conduct must play a substantial or motivating role, and an actual same-decision defense requires a preponderance, not a conceivable lawful reason. Harlow/Anderson and Elder preserve particularized conduct-date notice and a dispositive defense without mandatory merits-first sequencing. Later San Filippo, Umbehr, O'Hare, Crawford-El, Jeffries, Clanton, and this decision cannot establish notice for the August 16, 1993 refusal; San Filippo is persuasive 1994 circuit authority, not Supreme Court law.

No immediate partial liability judgment, ultimate immunity disposition, reinstatement, backpay, damages, repayment, mandatory hearing, trial, or discovery follows. A denial resting solely on the corrected categorical ground remains subject to remand. Published lower-court recitations support the Board admission and claim limits; original pleadings, agency files, minutes, and briefs are not reproduced and establish no additional participation, policymaking predicate, concession, or default.

### January 12, 1998 — Smith v. Mattox

[Public Projection](../records/smith_v_mattox_merits_1998-01-12.md#public-projection). Scalia's Parts I–II are unanimous. Part III's complete retained-immunity framework has all eight Associate Justices; Stone-Zsela joins its disposition and ordinary-proceedings limits. All nine affirm the denial of qualified-immunity summary judgment on the presented legal question and proper assumed facts, without finding final constitutional liability.

Johnson, Behrens, and Clanton permit a separable legal immunity inquiry on plaintiff-favorable historical assumptions while barring selection among accounts, credibility decisions, or factual-sufficiency review. Section 1254 enlarges no circuit §1291 authority. Unlike Clanton's separately identified factual challenges, no factual-sufficiency component requiring dismissal is established here. The assumed account is accepted solely for this appeal.

Graham requires objectively reasonable arrest force from the officer's contemporaneous perspective, considering crime severity, immediate threat, and active resistance or flight. The earlier bat threat, refusal, flight, and feigned renewed flight justified caution and some restraint; submission neither established completed cuffing nor prohibited every further use of force. On the complete assumed account, however, Smith was wholly unresisting during the maneuver, complained of discomfort from an arm pull, and experienced a grunt and blow followed by multiple fractures requiring surgery. That considerable additional force was obviously outside the remaining latitude for reasonable error under pre-June 11, 1993 law. The whole account, rather than injury alone, supplies obvious unlawfulness; no factually identical handcuffing-injury case is needed. Continued resistance, gun-reaching, or an unassumed safety mistake cannot be inserted into it.

Harlow and Anderson require particularized objective notice, not merely an abstract bodily-security right. Graham supplies the preconduct rule; Elder and Lanier explain relevant-law review and obvious coverage without retrospective notice or transfer of criminal elements. No exhaustive absence-of-authority finding or exclusive factually-similar-case method is adopted. Subjective anger is neither assumed nor necessary; bad purpose, hindsight, or a fracture alone is not decisive. Later Kelly and Post formulations supply no earlier notice.

Harlow/Anderson remains governing immunity law. Mattox may prove a materially different supported account, including continued resistance or necessary force, and obtain lawful fact-dependent reconsideration. Elder imports no new claims or evidence. Crawford-El preserves ordinary competent proof and independent defenses without adding a motive element or retaliation's burden sequence to Graham. Unpleaded entry and probable-cause theories, disposed city and chief claims, unlawful-arrest damages, and a separate Fourteenth Amendment claim remain outside the decision. No final violation, causal finding, supervisory liability, damages, compulsory trial, hearing, discovery plan, special interrogatory, or scheduled return follows. Original pleadings and depositions are not reproduced; no anger, purpose to break the arm, completed cuffing, missing evidence, waiver, or litigant default is inferred.

### January 13, 1998 — Baker v. General Motors Corp.

[Public Projection](../records/baker_v_general_motors_corp_merits_1998-01-13.md#public-projection). Stevens's Parts I and III have all nine Justices, with Part III's common conclusions supported through the respective writings' stated reasons. Part II's independently sufficient federal recognition/execution ground has Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Scalia, Souter, Ginsburg, and Breyer. Kennedy, joined by O'Connor and Thomas, concurs in Parts I and III and judgment on a distinct Michigan-effect ground. Reversal of mandatory exclusion and remand are unanimous; the six-vote federal rule controls directly without a Marks combination.

Section 1738 applies to the state consent judgment in this diversity forum. Consent, mere modifiability, or disagreement with local evidence policy does not permit withholding lawful rendering-state adjudicative effect; no general public-policy exception or Michigan modification is recognized. Matsushita preserves that effect and constitutional authority to bind represented persons. Richards requires a sufficient relationship to bind strangers' rights: shared interests or opportunity to intervene alone is insufficient, while consent, control, genuine privity, adequate representation, or another sufficient relationship may qualify. Neither decision makes every nonparty consequence invalid preclusion.

Part II distinguishes lawful adjudicative recognition from the requested evidentiary execution. The GM–Elwell decree governed their relations, not the Bakers' wrongful-death claims or their entitlement to invoke ordinary evidence process; it does not itself compel this separate forum to exclude testimony. A personal command capable of affecting others does not supply the additional exclusion duty GM seeks. The decree remains operative within its lawful sphere. This bounded rule excludes neither injunctions from §1738 nor every cross-border personal duty, allows neither modification of Michigan's order nor policy override of valid preclusion, and creates no universal witness-access or nonparty exception. Land decrees, antisuit injunctions, other foreign evidence orders, and Elwell's lawful personal enforcement remain outside the holding.

The Bakers' evidence request is not a modification request on the asserted basis and requires no Michigan proceeding. Celotex retains actual jurisdictional connection, sufficient authority, covered restraint, and lawful relief/review requirements, including the United Mine Workers and Walker qualifications; it supplies no missing authority to bind outsiders or classify their evidence request as disobedience. The injunction's exception concerns the specified then-pending Georgia case. The separate settlement agreement protects court-ordered testimony in other proceedings against an action for breach of the agreement or injunction. These instruments and exceptions remain distinct, without unquoted contractual terms, permission generally to disregard orders, or adjudication of personal enforcement.

The Eighth Circuit must proceed without compulsory exclusion under §1738, preserving actual privilege, trade-secret, work-product, admissibility, protective-order, and supported preclusion questions. The undisputed reported representation that GM made no such objection to a particular question or answer establishes neither universal lack of protection nor waiver of every objection. Other appellate grounds remain; the entire $11.3 million verdict is not automatically restored, and no new trial, blanket admission, relief against Elwell, or injunction dissolution follows. Matsushita's collateral-review reservation remains open. Original instruments, the complete transcript, and all appellate issues are not reproduced; the Michigan authorities' full opinions, omitted qualifications, and complete historical Rule 2.613(B) text and amendments are unavailable within this source projection, supplying no exhaustive exception rule or factual default.

### January 21, 1998 — Lunding v. New York Tax Appeals Tribunal

[Public Projection](../records/lunding_v_new_york_tax_appeals_tribunal_merits_1998-01-21.md#public-projection). The following complete public blocks preserve this event's action, proposition-specific authority, published positions, procedure and source limits.

#### Event

Lunding v. New York Tax Appeals Tribunal, No. 96-1462. Plenary merits decision, January 21, 1998; argued November 5, 1997. On writ of certiorari to the New York Court of Appeals, 89 N.Y.2d 283, 675 N.E.2d 816 (1996), which sustained N.Y. Tax Law § 631(b)(6). The question is whether denying qualifying alimony deductions in calculating nonresidents' New York-source income, within the complete nonresident tax formula, violates Article IV's Privileges and Immunities Clause. Equal Protection and Commerce Clause claims are outside this review.

Christopher and Barbara Lunding were Connecticut residents. Christopher earned income practicing law in New York and paid alimony to a Connecticut former spouse under a Connecticut decree. They claimed a proportionate deduction using approximately 48% of his business income as attributable to New York. Disallowance produced an additional assessment of $3,724 plus interest.

#### Participation

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer participate.

#### Public Action

The judgment of the New York Court of Appeals is reversed, and the case is remanded for nondiscriminatory treatment of the challenged assessment and appropriate relief under the governing state procedures and constitutional requirements.

#### Judgment & Remedy

**Reverse and remand, 7–2.**

**Supporting:** Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas and Breyer.
**Opposing:** Kennedy and Ginsburg.

State proceedings must provide lawful allocation and constitutionally adequate relief. If payment was compelled before a meaningful opportunity to contest the tax, adequate backward-looking correction is required. Independently, if the State clearly held out a postpayment remedy and payment occurred, it may not withdraw that remedy afterward, including for voluntary payments. Actual payment, preservation, amount and applicable procedural conditions remain to be established. No fixed refund, interest award or replacement formula is ordered.

#### Opinion Topology

Thomas delivers the opinion of the Court, joined in full by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter and Breyer. The same seven Justices adopt both holdings and all their qualifications. Ginsburg dissents, joined in full by Kennedy, on both the income-attribution and independent home-State personal-expense grounds.

#### Holdings

##### Article IV does not permit this inadequately justified categorical exclusion

**Holding and operative rule:** When a State taxes another State's citizens on income earned within its borders, a discriminatory deduction rule must rest on a substantial reason beyond noncitizenship, and the discrimination must bear a close relationship to that reason. Section 631(b)(6)'s categorical exclusion of qualifying alimony from deductions against New York-source income fails that inquiry on the source, personal-expense, initial-computation and family-income justifications offered here; genuine source allocation remains permissible, and identical resident and nonresident tax codes are not required.

**Authority:** The seven-Justice Opinion of the Court supplies direct majority authority for this rule, its application and the qualifications below.

**Controlling explanation:** Toomer protects substantial equality in earning a livelihood across state lines while allowing considerable state latitude. Shaffer permits taxation of local income and corresponding source-related deduction limits; Travis nevertheless rejects unjustified unequal personal allowances. Those decisions require examination of the particular deduction and tax, not ordinary rationality alone or mechanical equality.

New York taxes residents' worldwide income and nonresidents' local income. That real difference does not by itself justify excluding all proportionate recognition of alimony. The payment adjusts income available to the payer and lacks an exclusively Connecticut source merely because the former spouses and divorce decree are there. The lower court's Goodwin analogy involved geographically connected home-State property and medical expenses; it does not resolve this alimony provision. The Court adopts no rule requiring apportionment of every personal expense.

The whole calculation also matters. Qualifying alimony reduces hypothetical resident liability and the federal-adjusted-gross-income denominator, but § 631(b)(6) denies the deduction in the New York-source numerator. The initial benefit therefore does not establish substantial equality in the final burden. The ratio was uncapped; no particular actual ratio or total effective rate is found.

The asserted allocation of taxable family income likewise fails to match the enacted line. Payer residence determines the deduction without conditioning it on whether New York taxes the recipient. Examining that mismatch addresses the classification imposing the Lundings' own assessment, without finding how often different family arrangements occur. Neither a hypothetical recipient-based rule nor its constitutionality is decided.

Austin prevents another State's credit, deduction or fiscal choice from curing established discrimination. Connecticut's absence of earned-income taxation in this year supplies no cure. Fulton and Oregon Waste reinforce scrutiny of actual operation and unsupported offsets only by analogy; their Commerce Clause tests do not govern Article IV. States retain justified source limits and permissible cooperative tax arrangements.

##### Relief follows the established tax-remedy predicates

**Holding and operative rule:** The unconstitutional treatment of the challenged assessment requires reversal and remand for lawful state allocation, severability and constitutionally adequate relief. When the State compelled payment before meaningful review, it must provide adequate backward-looking correction; independently, a clearly held-out postpayment remedy cannot be withdrawn after payment, including voluntary payment, subject to ordinary proof and lawful previously announced conditions.

**Authority:** The same seven Justices join the Opinion of the Court's remedial holding in full.

**Controlling explanation:** McKesson requires meaningful correction when payment preceded an adequate opportunity to challenge; Harper distinguishes application of the governing federal rule from the lawful remedy. Fulton preserves state severability and remedial choices, including lawful refunds, constitutionally permissible nondiscriminatory retrospective equalization, or a lawful combination when backward-looking relief is required. Reich separately prevents a State from withdrawing a clear postpayment route after the taxpayer paid and substituting an unpursued prepayment route. Adequate prospective procedures remain permissible with fair notice.

The reported $3,724 additional assessment plus interest does not establish payment or an entitlement to that sum as a refund. The State must apply the constitutional rule through the procedures and payment facts actually established. The Court neither selects the taxpayers' proposed deduction formula as the exclusive remedy nor automatically restores every aspect of the Appellate Division's order. It orders no interest award, new assessment against others, or reopening of unrelated final tax years.

#### Precedent Treatment

- Toomer is applied for substantial equality, a substantial independent reason and the close relationship required between that reason and the discriminatory treatment.
- Shaffer retains nondiscriminatory source taxation and justified corresponding deduction limits; it supplies no categorical exception for every deduction labeled personal.
- Travis is applied to distinguish legitimate source limits from inadequately justified unequal personal allowances. Its rejection of presumed offsets remains operative.
- Austin is applied to reject a cure dependent on another State's tax choices; permissible cooperative arrangements remain available.
- Goodwin's reported home-State property and medical-expense setting is distinguished; that different deduction setting does not resolve this alimony provision.
- Friedsam's earlier statutory construction was superseded by § 631(b)(6); it is not revived as the constitutional rule or imposed as the exclusive replacement formula.
- Fulton and Oregon Waste supply analogical support concerning actual tax operation and unsupported offset explanations. Their Commerce Clause tests and Fulton's limited treatment of Darnell remain confined to their fields.
- McKesson, Harper, Fulton and Reich are applied to preserve conditional backward-looking correction, lawful state remedial choices and the independent protection of a clearly offered postpayment remedy.

#### Law After Decision

A State's narrower nonresident tax base and the personal character of a deduction do not automatically justify denying it. Courts must examine the challenged distinction in the complete tax scheme, require a substantial independent reason closely related to it, and preserve justified source allocation. Section 631(b)(6)'s categorical alimony exclusion cannot be sustained on the reasons offered here.

This decision adds that Article IV application without requiring identical deductions, strict numerical tax equality, the Commerce Clause's compensatory-tax elements or a particular replacement formula. It decides no Equal Protection or Commerce Clause claim, no dispute about federal alimony qualification, and no hypothetical recipient-based alternative. The existing conditional rules governing unconstitutional-tax relief remain in force.

#### Separate Writings

Ginsburg, joined by Kennedy, would affirm on two independent grounds.

First, the payer deduction and recipient inclusion allocate taxable income between former spouses. When both are New York residents, the State taxes the recipient and permits the payer's deduction; when both are Connecticut residents, denying the deduction preserves taxation of New York-source income that would otherwise pass outside the State's resident tax base. In their view, that source-tax and income-attribution relationship supplies a substantial justification in this case. Article IV requires substantial rather than mechanically precise equality. Imperfect treatment of other, mixed-residence couples does not establish these taxpayers' entitlement to remove the income from New York taxation. This position does not depend on a finding of aggregate interstate-payment balance or on Connecticut's imposing a tax.

Second, even treating alimony as an expense, the Connecticut payer, recipient and decree locate this particular marital obligation in the home State; it is not an expense of producing New York professional income. Shaffer and Travis permit the corresponding source limitation, and Goodwin's home-State expense analysis supports leaving that personal expense to Connecticut's law and policy. Travis's general personal exemption is different from a deduction for a particular nonlocal obligation. They also object that correlation with a payer's income does not distinguish alimony from many other personal expenditures; the Court's disclaimer of a universal deduction rule does not, in their view, resolve that distinction.

Their objection to relying on other former couples is a merits objection, not a vote to dismiss for lack of standing. They accept Austin's rule against curing established discrimination through another State's tax choices but find this allocation justified in the first place. They join neither the invalidity holding nor the correction mandate; their grounds are noncontrolling.

#### Procedure After Action

The case returns to the New York courts for proceedings consistent with the Article IV ruling, lawful allocation and state-law severability, and appropriate relief under applicable preserved procedures. Payment, amount, preservation and the actual remedial predicates must determine any backward-looking correction or monetary relief. No particular deduction formula, refund, interest award, compulsory hearing, retained Supreme Court jurisdiction or automatic further review is directed. This Supreme Court review is complete.

#### Source Notes

The challenged sentence and reported computation appear at 522 U.S. 291–292 & nn.1–2; taxpayer and state-proceeding facts appear at 293–296, with the Connecticut recipient and decree facts at 317 and 326. The state judgments are reported at 218 A.D.2d 268, 639 N.Y.S.2d 519, and 89 N.Y.2d 283, 675 N.E.2d 816. [Official report](https://tile.loc.gov/storage-services/service/ll/usrep/usrep522/usrep522287/usrep522287.pdf).

The reported record establishes an assessment but does not establish payment, complete return amounts, a particular actual apportionment percentage, or a refund or interest entitlement. The statutory ratio allowed percentages above 100%; that permission is not a finding that these taxpayers exceeded 100%. Connecticut's earned-income fact concerns these taxpayers and this tax year. No empirical finding about interstate household frequency or aggregate revenue parity supports the judgment.

### January 26, 1998 — Allentown Mack Sales & Service, Inc. v. NLRB

[Public Projection](../records/allentown_mack_sales_service_inc_v_nlrb_merits_1998-01-26.md#public-projection). The following complete public blocks preserve this event's action, proposition-specific authority, published positions, procedure and source limits.

#### Event

Allentown Mack Sales & Service, Inc. v. NLRB, No. 96-795. Plenary merits decision, January 26, 1998; argued October 15, 1997. On writ of certiorari to the United States Court of Appeals for the District of Columbia Circuit, 83 F.3d 1483 (1996), which enforced the Board's unfair-labor-practice and bargaining order. The question concerns the evidence an employer must possess before polling employees about an incumbent union's majority support, including the asserted substitution of proof of lost support for the announced reasonable-doubt standard.

Allentown rehired 32 of 45 former unit employees after purchasing the branch. The successor finding and appropriate unit are unchallenged. The ALJ found the procedural polling safeguards satisfied but found insufficient objective doubt. The later secret ballot was 19–13 against representation; that result does not establish what management knew when it announced the poll.

#### Participation

Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer participate.

#### Public Action

The judgment is reversed, and the case is remanded to the D.C. Circuit with instructions to deny enforcement of the Board order under review. A different majority sustains the facial validity of the Board's good-faith reasonable-doubt threshold for incumbent-union polling.

#### Judgment & Remedy

| Question or judgment component | Disposition and vote | Supporting Justices | Opposing Justices | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Facial challenge to the reasonable-doubt polling threshold | Rejected, 5–4 | Stevens, Scalia, Souter, Ginsburg, Breyer | Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Kennedy, Thomas | The announced threshold remains permissible; this is not a partial affirmance of enforcement |
| Substantial-evidence application and enforcement judgment | Reversed, 5–4 | Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas | Stevens, Souter, Ginsburg, Breyer | Remand to the D.C. Circuit with instructions to deny enforcement of the order under review |

The independent, unpreserved attack on the bargaining order's explanation and decertification bar is left unreviewed. Denial of enforcement rests on the preserved application challenge. The mandate permits no second weighing of this unchanged record or retroactive substitution of a new, more demanding threshold against Allentown. It orders no new poll, election, hearing, permanent decertification or determination of present employee preferences.

#### Opinion Topology
