[Public Projection](../records/richenberg_v_cohen_merits_1997-12-08.md#public-projection). Stevens, joined by Stone-Zsela, Souter, and Ginsburg, would reverse and set the discharge aside on an as-applied equality ground. The actual explanations inadequately connect ordinary attraction to likely prohibited conduct or explain rejection of the sworn undertaking and supporting evidence; receipt of rebuttal evidence is insufficient if the reasons deprive it of meaningful effect. Hypothetical lawful relationships and uncertainty about all future conduct do not supply the missing link. Military deference gives the Government's credibility reading weight but does not permit an appellate inference to replace the actual decision's required connection. The dissent preserves the policy and ordinary scrutiny, requires neither completed acts nor individual operational harm, and makes neither good service nor denial automatically dispositive. Lawful further military proceedings remain available without unconditional reinstatement, backpay, or resolution of every retention qualification. It adopts no independent APA, speech, or due-process ground.

Stone-Zsela's additional dissenting statement joins that equality ground and complete set-aside remedy, specifying that further military action must rest on a supported individualized finding of actual conduct, propensity, intent, or credibility under a lawful rule. Inability to predict every future relationship does not itself establish intent to violate military rules; additional explanation alone would not substitute for removing this discharge. Both dissenting positions are noncontrolling.

### City of Chicago — December 15, 1997

[Public Projection](../records/city_of_chicago_v_international_college_of_surgeons_merits_1997-12-15.md#public-projection). Ginsburg, joined by Stevens, would affirm because general grants do not sufficiently transfer Illinois's assigned appellate task of closed-record agency review with presumptively correct facts. An independent federal-rights suit differs; common facts, a federal issue, and possible later abstention do not answer the antecedent power objection. Stone-Zsela joins Part I and reversal but would additionally direct the District Court to recognize jurisdiction and then decide retention, stay, or remand under §1367(c), abstention, and remedial law. His instruction neither commands retention nor finds abstention warranted and does not control the appellate mandate.

### Clanton — December 29, 1997

[Public Projection](../records/clanton_v_cooper_merits_1997-12-29.md#public-projection). Stone-Zsela joins the legal/factual boundary and preserved deliberate-deception applications but would also affirm denial on the third surviving theory: use of a coerced manufactured confession against Clanton's own liberty concerns her due process, not borrowed interrogation rights. Falsity, coercion, materiality, and cause remain contestable; hearsay, absence of indictment, and charge timing create no independent liability. He also proposes a contextual defense with the justificatory burden on the official and no duplicative excuse for deliberate violations. Crawford-El did not adopt that change; it supplies no majority burden shift or procedure, and Harlow/Anderson remains governing law. His position makes neither denial of immunity nor assumed facts a liability finding or entitlement to trial.

### Valot — January 5, 1998

[Public Projection](../records/valot_v_southeast_local_school_district_board_of_education_merits_1998-01-05.md#public-projection). Stone-Zsela joins petition protection and the count-1 remand but would affirm every remaining disposition. His own formulation protects lawful, non-sham use of an established administrative-benefits process without public-concern content while retaining anti-fraud measures, eligibility contests, neutral filing rules, lawful prospective change, and independent employment grounds. He considers the specific Petition Clause source sufficient without separate substantive-due-process or equality relief and seeks adjudication of sole retaliatory causation, personal defenses, and remedy without inferring every defendant's liability. His broader formulation and different component remedies do not control.

O'Connor, joined by Scalia and Thomas, joins Part II but would affirm the federal defense judgment under the employee public-concern requirement. The applications sought personal income replacement; formality does not, for them, remove the employment dispute from that requirement. They rely neither solely on lack of renewal entitlement nor on a claim that successful petitioning cannot produce retaliation, presumed fraud, or a general fiscal license to punish independently protected conduct. Their petition and related-component positions are noncontrolling.

### Smith — January 12, 1998

[Public Projection](../records/smith_v_mattox_merits_1998-01-12.md#public-projection). Stone-Zsela joins Parts I–II and affirmance: the assumed absence of resistance, controlled position, and additional blow after complaint, rather than fracture alone, defeat the claimed legal immunity; earlier danger supplies no categorical authorization and disputed resistance cannot replace the assumed facts. He again proposes a contextual defense placing the justificatory burden on the official and rejecting a duplicative excuse for deliberate violations. The Court does not adopt that proposal, Crawford-El did not replace Harlow/Anderson, and the current objective defense remains intact. He preserves actual opportunities, reasonable mistakes, contrary supported facts, and remaining liability and remedial requirements. This proposed doctrinal change remains noncontrolling as in Clanton.

### Baker — January 13, 1998

[Public Projection](../records/baker_v_general_motors_corp_merits_1998-01-13.md#public-projection). Kennedy, joined by O'Connor and Thomas, finds Michigan's actual judgment-effect rules sufficient and withholds assent from the independent federal allocation ground. Nummer and Storey supply reported same-party, full-opportunity, and mutuality conditions; Lichon and Howell retain mutuality; Bacon and Detroit Railway give concrete nonparty-injunction applications; Qualls and E & G Finance place persuasion on the party invoking preclusion. The supported nonparty record supplies no bridge to compulsory exclusion, without invented privity, control, or exhaustive exceptions. A personal command may affect outsiders without precluding them, but does not itself supply the requested forum duty. Michigan Rule 2.613(B)'s reported modification direction neither changes this evidence request into modification nor requires a Michigan proceeding. Genuine personal enforcement and broader cross-border restraints remain reserved. This three-Justice rationale supplies no additional controlling rule.
